    q) 6/10 + 1/10   r) 8/11 + 1/11   s) 6/12 + 5/12   t) 2/7 + 3/7
    Answers: a) 2/3   b) 3/4   c) 3/5   d) 5/6   e) 6/7   f) 7/8   g) 8/9
             h) 9/10   i) 9/11   j) 11/12   k) 3/4   l) 2/5   m) 5/6   n) 3/7
             o) 7/8   p) 7/9   q) 7/10   r) 9/11   s) 11/12   t) 5/7

M2. Subtract. Keep the denominator.
    a) 2/3 − 1/3      b) 3/4 − 2/4      c) 2/5 − 1/5      d) 3/6 − 2/6
    e) 5/7 − 4/7      f) 6/8 − 3/8      g) 4/9 − 3/9      h) 4/10 − 3/10
    i) 6/11 − 1/11    j) 2/12 − 1/12    k) 2/4 − 1/4      l) 3/5 − 2/5
    m) 4/6 − 3/6      n) 3/7 − 2/7      o) 6/8 − 5/8      p) 5/9 − 3/9
    q) 2/10 − 1/10    r) 11/12 − 6/12   s) 5/9 − 5/9      t) 7/12 − 7/12
    u) 1/2 − 1/2      v) 4/4 − 1/4
    Answers: a) 1/3   b) 1/4   c) 1/5   d) 1/6   e) 1/7   f) 3/8   g) 1/9
             h) 1/10   i) 5/11   j) 1/12   k) 1/4   l) 1/5   m) 1/6   n) 1/7
             o) 1/8   p) 2/9   q) 1/10   r) 5/12   s) 0   t) 0   u) 0   v) 3/4

M3. Add. Write the answer as a mixed number or a whole number, and simplify.
    a) 1/2 + 1/2      b) 2/3 + 2/3      c) 2/4 + 3/4      d) 3/5 + 3/5
    e) 4/7 + 3/7      f) 3/7 + 6/7      g) 2/8 + 7/8      h) 7/9 + 8/9
    i) 5/10 + 7/10    j) 9/11 + 6/11    k) 10/12 + 6/12   l) 3/4 + 3/4
    m) 2/5 + 4/5      n) 3/6 + 4/6      o) 3/7 + 5/7      p) 7/8 + 3/8
    q) 8/9 + 8/9      r) 4/10 + 9/10    s) 8/11 + 7/11    t) 8/12 + 7/12
    Answers: a) 2/2 = 1   b) 4/3 = 1 1/3   c) 5/4 = 1 1/4   d) 6/5 = 1 1/5
             e) 7/7 = 1   f) 9/7 = 1 2/7   g) 9/8 = 1 1/8
             h) 15/9 = 1 6/9 = 1 2/3   i) 12/10 = 1 2/10 = 1 1/5
             j) 15/11 = 1 4/11   k) 16/12 = 1 4/12 = 1 1/3
             l) 6/4 = 1 2/4 = 1 1/2   m) 6/5 = 1 1/5   n) 7/6 = 1 1/6
             o) 8/7 = 1 1/7   p) 10/8 = 1 2/8 = 1 1/4   q) 16/9 = 1 7/9
             r) 13/10 = 1 3/10   s) 15/11 = 1 4/11   t) 15/12 = 1 3/12 = 1 1/4

M4. Work out, then simplify the answer.
    a) 1/4 + 1/4     b) 4/6 − 2/6     c) 2/8 + 2/8     d) 4/9 − 1/9
    e) 2/10 + 2/10   f) 4/12 − 2/12   g) 2/6 + 1/6     h) 6/8 − 2/8
    i) 2/9 + 4/9     j) 8/10 − 3/10   k) 1/12 + 5/12   l) 5/8 − 3/8
    m) 3/9 + 3/9     n) 6/10 − 1/10   o) 6/12 + 3/12   p) 5/6 − 2/6
    q) 4/10 + 4/10   r) 5/12 − 3/12
    Answers: a) 2/4 = 1/2   b) 2/6 = 1/3   c) 4/8 = 1/2   d) 3/9 = 1/3
             e) 4/10 = 2/5   f) 2/12 = 1/6   g) 3/6 = 1/2   h) 4/8 = 1/2
             i) 6/9 = 2/3   j) 5/10 = 1/2   k) 6/12 = 1/2   l) 2/8 = 1/4
             m) 6/9 = 2/3   n) 5/10 = 1/2   o) 9/12 = 3/4   p) 3/6 = 1/2
             q) 8/10 = 4/5   r) 2/12 = 1/6

M5. Take away from a whole number. (1 = 8/8, 2 = 1 4/4, and so on.)
    a) 1 − 1/2     b) 1 − 2/3     c) 1 − 1/4     d) 1 − 3/5     e) 1 − 5/6
    f) 1 − 4/7     g) 1 − 3/8     h) 1 − 2/9     i) 1 − 7/10    j) 1 − 6/11
    k) 1 − 5/12    l) 2 − 1/4     m) 3 − 2/5     n) 2 − 5/6     o) 4 − 3/8
    p) 5 − 7/10    q) 6 − 1/3     r) 10 − 1/2    s) 3 − 11/12   t) 2 − 2/4
    Answers: a) 1/2   b) 1/3   c) 3/4   d) 2/5   e) 1/6   f) 3/7   g) 5/8
             h) 7/9   i) 3/10   j) 5/11   k) 7/12   l) 1 3/4   m) 2 3/5
             n) 1 1/6   o) 3 5/8   p) 4 3/10   q) 5 2/3   r) 9 1/2   s) 2 1/12
             t) 1 2/4 = 1 1/2

M6. Add the wholes, then the fractions. Simplify if you can.
    a) 3 1/2 + 1         b) 6 1/3 + 4 1/3     c) 2 1/4 + 4 2/4
    d) 4 2/5 + 5 1/5     e) 4 1/6 + 4 3/6     f) 1 3/7 + 2 2/7
    g) 4 2/8 + 1 1/8     h) 2 5/9 + 2 3/9     i) 3 5/10 + 4 1/10
    j) 4 2/11 + 2 7/11   k) 5 1/12 + 4 7/12   l) 5 1/5 + 3 1/5
    m) 2 1/7 + 2 5/7     n) 4 5/8 + 4 2/8     o) 4 7/9 + 5 1/9
    p) 6 5/10 + 5 1/10
    Answers: a) 4 1/2   b) 10 2/3   c) 6 3/4   d) 9 3/5   e) 8 4/6 = 8 2/3
             f) 3 5/7   g) 5 3/8   h) 4 8/9   i) 7 6/10 = 7 3/5   j) 6 9/11
             k) 9 8/12 = 9 2/3   l) 8 2/5   m) 4 6/7   n) 8 7/8   o) 9 8/9
             p) 11 6/10 = 11 3/5

M7. Add. The fractions make more than 1, so carry a whole.
    a) 3 1/2 + 3 1/2     b) 4 2/3 + 4 1/3     c) 3 1/4 + 5 3/4
    d) 4 1/5 + 1 4/5     e) 2 5/6 + 3 3/6     f) 1 6/7 + 1 3/7
    g) 5 6/8 + 3 7/8     h) 2 6/9 + 3 7/9     i) 2 7/10 + 4 7/10
    j) 5 8/11 + 3 7/11   k) 2 3/12 + 1 9/12   l) 1 2/3 + 4 2/3
    m) 4 2/4 + 4 3/4     n) 1 3/5 + 2 2/5     o) 3 7/8 + 1 4/8
    p) 6 6/12 + 1 9/12
    Answers: a) 6 2/2 = 7   b) 8 3/3 = 9   c) 8 4/4 = 9   d) 5 5/5 = 6
             e) 5 8/6 = 6 2/6 = 6 1/3   f) 2 9/7 = 3 2/7   g) 8 13/8 = 9 5/8
             h) 5 13/9 = 6 4/9   i) 6 14/10 = 7 4/10 = 7 2/5
             j) 8 15/11 = 9 4/11   k) 3 12/12 = 4   l) 5 4/3 = 6 1/3
             m) 8 5/4 = 9 1/4   n) 3 5/5 = 4   o) 4 11/8 = 5 3/8
             p) 7 15/12 = 8 3/12 = 8 1/4

M8. Subtract the wholes, then the fractions. Simplify if you can.
    a) 4 1/2 − 3 1/2      b) 8 2/3 − 6 1/3      c) 5 1/4 − 2 1/4
    d) 5 3/5 − 4 3/5      e) 7 1/6 − 2 1/6      f) 8 1/7 − 5 1/7
    g) 8 5/8 − 2 3/8      h) 8 8/9 − 2 8/9      i) 3 1/10 − 2 1/10
    j) 9 2/11 − 4 2/11    k) 9 11/12 − 4 8/12   l) 4 3/5 − 3 3/5
    m) 4 2/6 − 2 1/6      n) 5 6/8 − 3 3/8      o) 4 4/10 − 1 2/10
    p) 7 9/12 − 4 7/12
    Answers: a) 1   b) 2 1/3   c) 3   d) 1   e) 5   f) 3   g) 6 2/8 = 6 1/4
             h) 6   i) 1   j) 5   k) 5 3/12 = 5 1/4   l) 1   m) 2 1/6   n) 2 3/8
             o) 3 2/10 = 3 1/5   p) 3 2/12 = 3 1/6

M9. Subtract. Regroup 1 whole first (3 1/4 = 2 5/4).
    a) 2 1/3 − 1 2/3      b) 3 1/4 − 1 3/4      c) 2 1/5 − 1 2/5
    d) 8 1/6 − 5 5/6      e) 3 3/7 − 1 5/7      f) 6 3/8 − 5 4/8
    g) 2 6/9 − 1 7/9      h) 5 4/10 − 2 9/10    i) 6 4/11 − 5 6/11
    j) 2 3/12 − 1 7/12    k) 6 1/4 − 5 3/4      l) 7 2/5 − 3 4/5
    m) 4 4/6 − 2 5/6      n) 7 2/8 − 1 7/8      o) 7 1/9 − 2 3/9
    p) 2 8/12 − 1 11/12
    Answers:
      a) 1 4/3 − 1 2/3 = 2/3
      b) 2 5/4 − 1 3/4 = 1 2/4 = 1 1/2
      c) 1 6/5 − 1 2/5 = 4/5
      d) 7 7/6 − 5 5/6 = 2 2/6 = 2 1/3
      e) 2 10/7 − 1 5/7 = 1 5/7
      f) 5 11/8 − 5 4/8 = 7/8
      g) 1 15/9 − 1 7/9 = 8/9
      h) 4 14/10 − 2 9/10 = 2 5/10 = 2 1/2
      i) 5 15/11 − 5 6/11 = 9/11
      j) 1 15/12 − 1 7/12 = 8/12 = 2/3
      k) 5 5/4 − 5 3/4 = 2/4 = 1/2
      l) 6 7/5 − 3 4/5 = 3 3/5
      m) 3 10/6 − 2 5/6 = 1 5/6
      n) 6 10/8 − 1 7/8 = 5 3/8
      o) 6 10/9 − 2 3/9 = 4 7/9
      p) 1 20/12 − 1 11/12 = 9/12 = 3/4

M10. Take away from a whole number. Write the whole as a mixed number first
     (5 = 4 8/8).
     a) 5 − 2 3/8     b) 4 − 1 2/3     c) 3 − 1 1/2     d) 6 − 3 3/4
     e) 2 − 1 4/5     f) 7 − 4 1/6     g) 10 − 5 5/7    h) 8 − 2 7/9
     i) 9 − 6 3/10    j) 3 − 2 10/11   k) 5 − 5/12      l) 4 − 1/2
     m) 2 − 3/8       n) 6 − 5 1/9     o) 1 − 4/11      p) 12 − 7 2/3
     Answers:
       a) 4 8/8 − 2 3/8 = 2 5/8
       b) 3 3/3 − 1 2/3 = 2 1/3
       c) 2 2/2 − 1 1/2 = 1 1/2
       d) 5 4/4 − 3 3/4 = 2 1/4
       e) 1 5/5 − 1 4/5 = 1/5
       f) 6 6/6 − 4 1/6 = 2 5/6
       g) 9 7/7 − 5 5/7 = 4 2/7
       h) 7 9/9 − 2 7/9 = 5 2/9
       i) 8 10/10 − 6 3/10 = 2 7/10
       j) 2 11/11 − 2 10/11 = 1/11
       k) 4 12/12 − 5/12 = 4 7/12
       l) 3 2/2 − 1/2 = 3 1/2
       m) 1 8/8 − 3/8 = 1 5/8
       n) 5 9/9 − 5 1/9 = 8/9
       o) 11/11 − 4/11 = 7/11
       p) 11 3/3 − 7 2/3 = 4 1/3

M11. Fill in the missing number.
     a) 3/8 + __/8 = 7/8         b) __/5 − 2/5 = 1/5
     c) 1 − __/6 = 1/6           d) __/9 + 4/9 = 1
     e) 7/10 − __/10 = 2/10      f) 5/12 + 5/12 = __/12
     g) 2/3 + __/3 = 1 2/3       h) 1 3/7 + __/7 = 2
     i) 2 3/7 + 1 __/7 = 4 1/7   j) 4 5/6 − __ 2/6 = 1 3/6
     k) __ − 3/4 = 1/4           l) 9/11 − 9/11 = __
     m) 3 − __/5 = 2 1/5         n) 1 7/8 + __ 1/8 = 5
     o) __/4 + 3/4 = 2           p) 6 1/3 − 2 __/3 = 3 2/3
     Answers: a) 4   b) 3   c) 5   d) 5   e) 5   f) 10   g) 3   h) 4   i) 5
              j) 3   k) 1   l) 0   m) 4   n) 3   o) 5   p) 2

M12. Work from left to right. Simplify the answer.
     a) 1/9 + 2/9 + 4/9             b) 7/8 − 2/8 − 1/8
     c) 3/10 + 9/10 − 5/10          d) 2/5 + 2/5 + 2/5
     e) 11/12 − 5/12 + 1/12         f) 1/6 + 1/6 + 1/6 + 1/6
     g) 4/7 + 5/7 + 6/7             h) 1 − 1/4 − 1/4
     i) 1 1/4 + 2 3/4 + 1/4         j) 2 1/3 + 1 1/3 + 1 1/3
     k) 5 − 1 1/2 − 1 1/2           l) 3 3/10 + 2 9/10 − 1 1/10
     m) 1/8 + 3/8 + 5/8 + 7/8       n) 4 5/9 − 1 7/9 + 2/9
     o) 2 − 1/3 − 1/3 − 1/3         p) 1/12 + 2/12 + 3/12 + 4/12
     Answers:
       a) 7/9
       b) 4/8 = 1/2
       c) 7/10
       d) 6/5 = 1 1/5
       e) 7/12
       f) 4/6 = 2/3
       g) 15/7 = 2 1/7
       h) 2/4 = 1/2
       i) 17/4 = 4 1/4
       j) 15/3 = 5
       k) 4/2 = 2
       l) 51/10 = 5 1/10
       m) 16/8 = 2
       n) 27/9 = 3
       o) 3/3 = 1
       p) 10/12 = 5/6

TEACHING TIPS
  Struggling: use fraction strips or a paper bar with the same number of parts
  every time, and say the denominator as a word ("3 eighths and 2 eighths make 5
  eighths") so the child sees that eighths do not change; practise regrouping 1
  whole as 4/4, 5/5, 8/8 with real pieces before mixed-number subtraction.
  Harder: longer chains of like fractions, missing-number puzzles like
  3 __/9 − 1 7/9 = 1 5/9, magic squares with fractions, and checking every
  subtraction by adding.


================================================================================
TOPIC 4.24 — MULTIPLYING A FRACTION BY A WHOLE NUMBER
================================================================================
WHAT THE CHILD MUST LEARN
  - 3 × 2/5 means 3 groups of 2/5: 2/5 + 2/5 + 2/5 = 6/5. It is repeated
    addition, just like 3 × 2 = 2 + 2 + 2.
  - Rule: multiply the NUMERATOR by the whole number and keep the denominator:
    3 × 2/5 = (3 × 2)/5 = 6/5. The size of the pieces (fifths) does not change;
    we just have more of them.
  - The order does not matter: 3 × 2/5 = 2/5 × 3.
  - Write improper answers as mixed numbers and simplify:
    4 × 3/8 = 12/8 = 1 4/8 = 1 1/2.
  - Multiplying by the denominator gives whole numbers: 5 × 1/5 = 5/5 = 1 and
    6 × 2/3 = 12/3 = 4.
  - "Of" means multiply: 3/4 of 20 = 3/4 × 20. Divide by the denominator, then
    multiply by the numerator: 20 ÷ 4 = 5, 5 × 3 = 15.
  - See it on a number line (4 jumps of 2/3 land on 8/3) and as an area model (3
    bars, each with 3 of 4 parts shaded, show 9/4).
  - Multiplying a number by a proper fraction gives LESS than the number:
    4 × 2/3 = 2 2/3, which is less than 4.

PART A — DESCRIPTIVE
--------------------
D1. Priya drinks 2/5 of a litre of milk every day. How much milk does she drink
    in 3 days?
    Answer: 6/5 = 1 1/5 litres. 3 × 2/5 = 2/5 + 2/5 + 2/5 = 6/5.
    Teacher explains: 3 days means 3 lots of 2/5. Write it as repeated addition:
    2/5 + 2/5 + 2/5. Adding like fractions, add the tops: 2 + 2 + 2 = 6, so 6/5.
    That is the same as 3 × 2 = 6 on top, with the 5 staying at the bottom:
    3 × 2/5 = 6/5. 6 ÷ 5 is 1 remainder 1, so 6/5 = 1 1/5 litres. Now you try:
    4 × 2/7. (8/7 = 1 1/7)

D2. Sonu says 3 × 2/5 = 6/15 because "you multiply the top and the bottom by 3".
    Is he right?
    Answer: No. 3 × 2/5 = 6/5. Only the numerator is multiplied.
    Teacher explains: Ask: is 3 × 2/5 more or less than 2/5? Three lots of
    something must be MORE than one lot. But 6/15 = 2/5 (divide top and bottom
    by 3), so Sonu's answer is exactly the same as ONE lot. Multiplying top and
    bottom by 3 makes an equivalent fraction, not 3 times as much. With 3 groups
    of 2 fifths we have 6 fifths: the pieces are still fifths. Now you try:
    4 × 1/9. (4/9)

D3. A frog jumps 2/3 of a metre with every jump. Where is it after 4 jumps, if
    it starts at 0?
                  1         2         3         4
        |----|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|
        0   1/3  2/3   1   4/3  5/3   2   7/3  8/3   3
        (the numbers above show where each jump lands)
    Answer: At 8/3 = 2 2/3 metres. 4 × 2/3 = 8/3.
    Teacher explains: Draw a number line in thirds. Each jump covers 2 thirds: 0
    → 2/3 → 4/3 → 6/3 → 8/3. After 4 jumps the frog is at 8/3. 8 ÷ 3 is 2
    remainder 2, so 8/3 = 2 2/3 m, two marks after 2. The line shows that
    4 × 2/3 is 4 equal jumps. Now you try: 5 jumps of 3/4. (15/4 = 3 3/4)

D4. Draw a picture to show 3 × 3/4.
        [■■■□] [■■■□] [■■■□]
          3/4    3/4    3/4
    Answer: 3 × 3/4 = 9/4 = 2 1/4.
    Teacher explains: Draw 3 bars, each cut into 4 equal parts, and shade 3
    parts in each bar. Count the shaded quarters: 3 + 3 + 3 = 9 quarters, 9/4.
    Now move the shaded parts to fill whole bars: 4 quarters fill the first bar,
    4 more fill the second, and 1 quarter is left: 2 1/4. The picture is an area
    model of 3 × 3/4. Now you try: draw 2 × 2/3. (4/3 = 1 1/3)

D5. A pizza is cut into 8 slices. Each of 4 children eats 3/8 of a pizza. How
    much pizza do they eat altogether?
    Answer: 1 1/2 pizzas. 4 × 3/8 = 12/8 = 1 4/8 = 1 1/2.
    Teacher explains: 4 × 3 = 12 eighths: 12/8. 12 ÷ 8 is 1 remainder 4, so
    12/8 = 1 4/8. The fraction 4/8 simplifies to 1/2 (half the pizza), so the
    answer is 1 1/2 pizzas. You can also simplify first: 12/8 = 3/2 = 1 1/2.
    Always ask "Can I make it simpler?" Now you try: 6 × 3/10.
    (18/10 = 1 8/10 = 1 4/5)

D6. Mummy cuts ribbon into 6 pieces, each 2/3 of a metre long. How much ribbon
    did she use?
    Answer: 4 metres. 6 × 2/3 = 12/3 = 4.
    Teacher explains: 6 × 2 = 12 thirds, 12/3. 12 ÷ 3 = 4 with no remainder, so
    the answer is a whole number: 4 m. Why? 3 pieces of 2/3 m make 6/3 = 2 m,
    and 6 pieces make twice as much. When the whole number is a multiple of the
    denominator, the answer is always a whole number: 5 × 1/5 = 1, 8 × 3/4 = 6.
    Now you try: 10 × 2/5. (20/5 = 4)

D7. Dadi made 20 laddoos. She gives 3/4 of them to the neighbours. How many
    laddoos does she give?
        [● ● ● ● ●] [● ● ● ● ●] [● ● ● ● ●] [○ ○ ○ ○ ○]
          given        given       given       kept
    Answer: 15 laddoos. 3/4 of 20 = (20 ÷ 4) × 3 = 15.
    Teacher explains: First find 1/4 of 20: share into 4 equal groups,
    20 ÷ 4 = 5. 3/4 means 3 of those groups: 5 × 3 = 15. Another way: 3/4 of
    20 = 3/4 × 20 = (3 × 20)/4 = 60/4 = 15. The first way (divide first) keeps
    the numbers small. "Of" means multiply. Now you try: 2/3 of 18. (12)

D8. Rahul has ₹350. He spends 2/5 of it on a cricket ball. How much does the
    ball cost? How much money is left?
    Answer: The ball costs ₹140. ₹210 is left.
    Teacher explains: 1/5 of 350: 350 ÷ 5 = 70. 2/5 is two of these:
    70 × 2 = 140. So the ball costs ₹140. He has 5/5 − 2/5 = 3/5 of his money
    left: 70 × 3 = 210. Check: 140 + 210 = 350. Draw a bar of ₹350 cut into 5
    equal parts of ₹70. Now you try: 3/10 of ₹500. (₹150)

D9. The school lunch break is 5/6 of an hour. How many minutes is that?
    Answer: 50 minutes. 5/6 of 60 = (60 ÷ 6) × 5 = 50.
    Teacher explains: An hour is 60 minutes. 1/6 of 60 is 60 ÷ 6 = 10 minutes,
    so 5/6 is 5 × 10 = 50 minutes. Fractions of an hour are useful: 1/4 hour =
    15 minutes, 1/2 hour = 30 minutes, 3/4 hour = 45 minutes, 1/3 hour = 20
    minutes. Now you try: 2/3 of an hour. (40 minutes)

D10. A packet holds 1 1/4 kg of flour. How much flour is in 3 packets?
     Answer: 3 3/4 kg. 3 × 1 1/4 = 3 × 5/4 = 15/4 = 3 3/4.
     Teacher explains: Way 1: change the mixed number to an improper fraction:
     1 1/4 = 5/4. Then 3 × 5/4 = 15/4 = 3 3/4. Way 2: multiply the whole and the
     fraction separately and add: 3 × 1 = 3 and 3 × 1/4 = 3/4, so
     3 + 3/4 = 3 3/4. Both give 3 3/4 kg. A common mistake is to multiply only
     the whole number: 3 × 1 1/4 is NOT 3 1/4. Now you try: 2 × 2 1/3. (4 2/3)

D11. Without working it out, is 4 × 2/3 more or less than 4? Is 4 × 5/3 more or
     less than 4?
     Answer: 4 × 2/3 is less than 4 (it is 2 2/3). 4 × 5/3 is more than 4 (it is
     6 2/3).
     Teacher explains: 4 × 1 = 4. 2/3 is less than 1, so 4 lots of 2/3 must be
     less than 4 lots of 1: 4 × 2/3 = 8/3 = 2 2/3 < 4. 5/3 is more than 1, so
     4 × 5/3 = 20/3 = 6 2/3 > 4. Multiplying by a proper fraction makes a number
     smaller; this surprises many children, who think "multiplying always makes
     bigger". Now you try: is 6 × 3/4 more or less than 6? (less: 4 1/2)

D12. One batch of halwa needs 3/4 cup of sugar. Amma makes 5 batches for Diwali.
     How much sugar does she need?
     Answer: 3 3/4 cups. 5 × 3/4 = 15/4 = 3 3/4.
     Teacher explains: 5 × 3 = 15 quarters: 15/4. 15 ÷ 4 is 3 remainder 3, so
     3 3/4 cups. Estimate first: 3/4 is almost 1, so 5 batches need almost 5
     cups, but a bit less. 3 3/4 is sensible. Now you try: 4 batches of 2/3 cup
     of ghee. (8/3 = 2 2/3 cups)

PART B — MATHEMATICAL ONLY
--------------------------
M1. Write as repeated addition, then find the answer.
    a) 3 × 2/5    b) 2 × 3/7    c) 4 × 1/3    d) 3 × 3/8
    e) 5 × 2/9    f) 2 × 5/6    g) 4 × 3/10   h) 3 × 4/5
    i) 5 × 1/4    j) 2 × 7/12   k) 6 × 1/2    l) 4 × 2/11
    Answers:
      a) 2/5 + 2/5 + 2/5 = 6/5 = 1 1/5
      b) 3/7 + 3/7 = 6/7
      c) 1/3 + 1/3 + 1/3 + 1/3 = 4/3 = 1 1/3
      d) 3/8 + 3/8 + 3/8 = 9/8 = 1 1/8
      e) 2/9 + 2/9 + 2/9 + 2/9 + 2/9 = 10/9 = 1 1/9
      f) 5/6 + 5/6 = 10/6 = 1 4/6 = 1 2/3
      g) 3/10 + 3/10 + 3/10 + 3/10 = 12/10 = 1 2/10 = 1 1/5
      h) 4/5 + 4/5 + 4/5 = 12/5 = 2 2/5
      i) 1/4 + 1/4 + 1/4 + 1/4 + 1/4 = 5/4 = 1 1/4
      j) 7/12 + 7/12 = 14/12 = 1 2/12 = 1 1/6
      k) 1/2 + 1/2 + 1/2 + 1/2 + 1/2 + 1/2 = 6/2 = 3
      l) 2/11 + 2/11 + 2/11 + 2/11 = 8/11

M2. Multiply.
    a) 2 × 1/3    b) 3 × 1/4    c) 5 × 1/5    d) 7 × 1/10   e) 4 × 1/2
    f) 6 × 1/4    g) 9 × 1/12   h) 8 × 1/3    i) 3 × 1/8    j) 10 × 1/5
    k) 2 × 1/9    l) 5 × 1/6    m) 7 × 1/7    n) 11 × 1/4   o) 4 × 1/11
    p) 12 × 1/6   q) 3 × 1/2    r) 6 × 1/9    s) 1 × 1/8    t) 0 × 1/5
    Answers: a) 2/3   b) 3/4   c) 5/5 = 1   d) 7/10   e) 4/2 = 2
             f) 6/4 = 1 2/4 = 1 1/2   g) 9/12 = 3/4   h) 8/3 = 2 2/3   i) 3/8
             j) 10/5 = 2   k) 2/9   l) 5/6   m) 7/7 = 1   n) 11/4 = 2 3/4
             o) 4/11   p) 12/6 = 2   q) 3/2 = 1 1/2   r) 6/9 = 2/3   s) 1/8
             t) 0

M3. Multiply. (The answers are less than 1.)
    a) 2 × 1/5    b) 3 × 1/5    c) 2 × 3/7    d) 3 × 2/7    e) 2 × 1/7
    f) 3 × 1/8    g) 2 × 2/9    h) 4 × 2/9    i) 3 × 1/10   j) 3 × 3/10
    k) 2 × 5/11   l) 3 × 3/11   m) 4 × 2/11   n) 5 × 1/12   o) 7 × 1/12
    p) 4 × 1/9    q) 2 × 1/3    r) 3 × 1/4
    Answers: a) 2/5   b) 3/5   c) 6/7   d) 6/7   e) 2/7   f) 3/8   g) 4/9
             h) 8/9   i) 3/10   j) 9/10   k) 10/11   l) 9/11   m) 8/11   n) 5/12
             o) 7/12   p) 4/9   q) 2/3   r) 3/4

M4. Multiply. Write the answer as a mixed number or a whole number.
    a) 9 × 1/2     b) 2 × 2/3     c) 4 × 3/4     d) 8 × 2/5     e) 3 × 4/6
    f) 5 × 6/7     g) 8 × 3/8     h) 8 × 8/9     i) 9 × 7/10    j) 2 × 10/11
    k) 6 × 8/12    l) 5 × 1/3     m) 6 × 2/4     n) 9 × 4/5     o) 9 × 4/7
    p) 9 × 1/8     q) 8 × 5/9     r) 7 × 10/11   s) 3 × 4/5     t) 9 × 1/3
    Answers: a) 9/2 = 4 1/2   b) 4/3 = 1 1/3   c) 12/4 = 3   d) 16/5 = 3 1/5
             e) 12/6 = 2   f) 30/7 = 4 2/7   g) 24/8 = 3   h) 64/9 = 7 1/9
             i) 63/10 = 6 3/10   j) 20/11 = 1 9/11   k) 48/12 = 4
             l) 5/3 = 1 2/3   m) 12/4 = 3   n) 36/5 = 7 1/5   o) 36/7 = 5 1/7
             p) 9/8 = 1 1/8   q) 40/9 = 4 4/9   r) 70/11 = 6 4/11
             s) 12/5 = 2 2/5   t) 9/3 = 3

M5. Multiply, then simplify.
    a) 2 × 1/4     b) 2 × 4/6     c) 5 × 4/8     d) 6 × 2/9     e) 2 × 6/10
    f) 3 × 5/12    g) 5 × 2/4     h) 2 × 1/6     i) 5 × 2/8     j) 3 × 8/10
    k) 4 × 8/12    l) 4 × 2/6     m) 4 × 7/8     n) 6 × 7/9     o) 3 × 6/10
    p) 6 × 11/12   q) 3 × 2/8     r) 3 × 9/12
    Answers: a) 2/4 = 1/2   b) 8/6 = 1 2/6 = 1 1/3   c) 20/8 = 2 4/8 = 2 1/2
             d) 12/9 = 1 3/9 = 1 1/3   e) 12/10 = 1 2/10 = 1 1/5
             f) 15/12 = 1 3/12 = 1 1/4   g) 10/4 = 2 2/4 = 2 1/2   h) 2/6 = 1/3
             i) 10/8 = 1 2/8 = 1 1/4   j) 24/10 = 2 4/10 = 2 2/5
             k) 32/12 = 2 8/12 = 2 2/3   l) 8/6 = 1 2/6 = 1 1/3
             m) 28/8 = 3 4/8 = 3 1/2   n) 42/9 = 4 6/9 = 4 2/3
             o) 18/10 = 1 8/10 = 1 4/5   p) 66/12 = 5 6/12 = 5 1/2
             q) 6/8 = 3/4   r) 27/12 = 2 3/12 = 2 1/4

M6. Multiply. Compare each pair: does the order matter?
    a) 2/7 × 3    b) 3 × 2/7    c) 1/6 × 5    d) 5 × 1/6
    e) 3/5 × 4    f) 4 × 3/5    g) 5/8 × 2    h) 2 × 5/8
    i) 2/9 × 6    j) 6 × 2/9    k) 3/10 × 7   l) 7 × 3/10
    m) 5/12 × 3   n) 3 × 5/12   o) 1/3 × 8    p) 8 × 1/3
    Answers: a) 6/7   b) 6/7   c) 5/6   d) 5/6   e) 12/5 = 2 2/5
             f) 12/5 = 2 2/5   g) 10/8 = 1 2/8 = 1 1/4   h) 10/8 = 1 2/8 = 1 1/4
             i) 12/9 = 1 3/9 = 1 1/3   j) 12/9 = 1 3/9 = 1 1/3
             k) 21/10 = 2 1/10   l) 21/10 = 2 1/10   m) 15/12 = 1 3/12 = 1 1/4
             n) 15/12 = 1 3/12 = 1 1/4   o) 8/3 = 2 2/3   p) 8/3 = 2 2/3

M7. Find the fraction of the number.
    a) 1/2 of 18      b) 1/3 of 27      c) 1/4 of 36      d) 1/5 of 35
    e) 1/6 of 42      f) 1/7 of 49      g) 1/8 of 64      h) 1/9 of 81
    i) 1/10 of 70     j) 1/11 of 55     k) 1/12 of 96     l) 1/2 of 150
    m) 1/3 of 120     n) 1/4 of 200     o) 1/5 of 1000    p) 1/6 of 300
    q) 1/8 of 400     r) 1/10 of 1000   s) 1/12 of 60     t) 1/7 of 700
    Answers: a) 9   b) 9   c) 9   d) 7   e) 7   f) 7   g) 8   h) 9   i) 7   j) 5
             k) 8   l) 75   m) 40   n) 50   o) 200   p) 50   q) 50   r) 100
             s) 5   t) 100

M8. Find the fraction of the number. Divide by the denominator, then multiply by
    the numerator.
    a) 3/4 of 20     b) 2/3 of 18     c) 2/5 of 35     d) 5/6 of 42
    e) 3/8 of 40     f) 4/7 of 28     g) 7/10 of 90    h) 5/9 of 63
    i) 3/11 of 44    j) 7/12 of 84    k) 3/5 of 100    l) 5/8 of 72
    m) 2/9 of 108    n) 9/10 of 200   o) 11/12 of 36   p) 4/5 of 45
    q) 3/4 of 1000   r) 5/7 of 140    s) 1/2 of 250    t) 6/6 of 30
    Answers:
      a) (20 ÷ 4) × 3 = 15
      b) (18 ÷ 3) × 2 = 12
      c) (35 ÷ 5) × 2 = 14
      d) (42 ÷ 6) × 5 = 35
      e) (40 ÷ 8) × 3 = 15
      f) (28 ÷ 7) × 4 = 16
      g) (90 ÷ 10) × 7 = 63
      h) (63 ÷ 9) × 5 = 35
      i) (44 ÷ 11) × 3 = 12
      j) (84 ÷ 12) × 7 = 49
      k) (100 ÷ 5) × 3 = 60
      l) (72 ÷ 8) × 5 = 45
      m) (108 ÷ 9) × 2 = 24
      n) (200 ÷ 10) × 9 = 180
      o) (36 ÷ 12) × 11 = 33
      p) (45 ÷ 5) × 4 = 36
      q) (1000 ÷ 4) × 3 = 750
      r) (140 ÷ 7) × 5 = 100
      s) (250 ÷ 2) × 1 = 125
      t) (30 ÷ 6) × 6 = 30

M9. Fraction of a measure. (1 m = 100 cm, 1 kg = 1000 g, 1 km = 1000 m,
    1 L = 1000 mL, 1 hour = 60 min, 1 day = 24 hours, 1 year = 12 months,
    ₹1 = 100 paise.)
    a) 3/4 of 100 cm = __ cm        b) 1/4 of 60 min = __ min
    c) 2/5 of ₹100 = ₹__            d) 3/10 of 1000 g = __ g
    e) 5/6 of 60 min = __ min       f) 7/10 of 1 m = __ cm
    g) 3/4 of 1 kg = __ g           h) 1/3 of 1 hour = __ min
    i) 2/3 of 1 day = __ hours      j) 3/8 of 1000 m = __ m
    k) 5/12 of 1 year = __ months   l) 3/5 of ₹1 = __ paise
    m) 1/2 of 1 L = __ mL           n) 9/10 of 1 km = __ m
    o) 3/4 of 1 hour = __ min       p) 4/5 of ₹50 = ₹__
    Answers: a) 75   b) 15   c) 40   d) 300   e) 50   f) 70   g) 750   h) 20
             i) 16   j) 375   k) 5   l) 60   m) 500   n) 900   o) 45   p) 40

M10. Start at 0 on a number line. Where do you land? Write the multiplication
     and the answer.
     Example: 3 jumps of 3/4 on a line marked in quarters.
                 1           2           3
     |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
     0               1               2               3
     3 × 3/4 = 9/4 = 2 1/4

     a) 4 jumps of 2/3    b) 5 jumps of 3/4    c) 3 jumps of 1/2
     d) 6 jumps of 1/3    e) 2 jumps of 5/6    f) 7 jumps of 2/5
     g) 3 jumps of 3/8    h) 4 jumps of 5/4    i) 8 jumps of 3/4
     j) 9 jumps of 2/3    k) 5 jumps of 7/10   l) 10 jumps of 1/4
     Answers:
       a) 4 × 2/3 = 8/3 = 2 2/3
       b) 5 × 3/4 = 15/4 = 3 3/4
       c) 3 × 1/2 = 3/2 = 1 1/2
       d) 6 × 1/3 = 6/3 = 2
       e) 2 × 5/6 = 10/6 = 1 4/6 = 1 2/3
       f) 7 × 2/5 = 14/5 = 2 4/5
       g) 3 × 3/8 = 9/8 = 1 1/8
       h) 4 × 5/4 = 20/4 = 5
       i) 8 × 3/4 = 24/4 = 6
       j) 9 × 2/3 = 18/3 = 6
       k) 5 × 7/10 = 35/10 = 3 5/10 = 3 1/2
       l) 10 × 1/4 = 10/4 = 2 2/4 = 2 1/2

M11. Each bar is 1 whole. Write the multiplication shown, and the answer.
     a) [■■■□] [■■■□]
     b) [■■□] [■■□] [■■□]
     c) [■□] [■□] [■□] [■□]
     d) [■■■■□] [■■■■□]
     e) [■■■■■□] [■■■■■□] [■■■■■□]
     f) [■■□] [■■□] [■■□] [■■□] [■■□]
     g) [■■■■■■■□] [■■■■■■■□]
     h) [■■■□□] [■■■□□] [■■■□□] [■■■□□]
     i) [■□□□] [■□□□] [■□□□]
     j) [■□□] [■□□] [■□□] [■□□] [■□□] [■□□]
     k) [■■■■■□] [■■■■■□]
     l) [■■■■□□] [■■■■□□] [■■■■□□]
     Answers:
       a) 2 × 3/4 = 6/4 = 1 2/4 = 1 1/2
       b) 3 × 2/3 = 6/3 = 2
       c) 4 × 1/2 = 4/2 = 2
       d) 2 × 4/5 = 8/5 = 1 3/5
       e) 3 × 5/6 = 15/6 = 2 3/6 = 2 1/2
       f) 5 × 2/3 = 10/3 = 3 1/3
       g) 2 × 7/8 = 14/8 = 1 6/8 = 1 3/4
       h) 4 × 3/5 = 12/5 = 2 2/5
       i) 3 × 1/4 = 3/4
       j) 6 × 1/3 = 6/3 = 2
       k) 2 × 5/6 = 10/6 = 1 4/6 = 1 2/3
       l) 3 × 4/6 = 12/6 = 2

M12. Fill in the missing number, or write <, > or =. For a whole number times a
     mixed number, multiply the whole and the fraction separately
     (4 × 1 1/2 = 4 + 4/2).
     a) __ × 2/5 = 8/5     b) 3 × __/7 = 6/7     c) 5 × 1/__ = 5/8
     d) __ × 1/4 = 2       e) 7 × 1/7 = __       f) 10 × 3/10 = __
     g) 4 × 1 1/2 = __     h) 3 × 2 1/3 = __     i) 2 × 3 3/4 = __
     j) 5 × 1 2/5 = __     k) 5 × 2/3 __ 3       l) 6 × 5/6 __ 5
     m) 4 × 3/4 __ 4       n) 2 × 7/8 __ 1 3/4   o) 8 × 1/2 __ 3
     p) 9 × 2/9 __ 3
     Answers: a) 4   b) 2   c) 8   d) 8   e) 1   f) 3   g) 4 + 4/2 = 6
              h) 6 + 3/3 = 7   i) 6 + 6/4 = 7 2/4 = 7 1/2   j) 5 + 10/5 = 7
              k) 5 × 2/3 > 3   l) 6 × 5/6 = 5   m) 4 × 3/4 < 4
              n) 2 × 7/8 = 1 3/4   o) 8 × 1/2 > 3   p) 9 × 2/9 < 3

TEACHING TIPS
  Struggling: always say it as groups ("3 lots of 2 fifths") and build it with
  fraction strips or paper pieces before writing; do repeated addition first and
  let the child notice the shortcut; for fraction of a quantity use counters
  shared onto plates. Harder: whole numbers times mixed numbers (7 × 2 3/8),
  finding the missing whole number (__ × 3/4 = 6), fractions of larger
  quantities (3/8 of 1000), and explaining why 5 × 3/4 is less than 5.


================================================================================
TOPIC 4.25 — DECIMALS: TENTHS AND HUNDREDTHS; LINK TO FRACTIONS AND MONEY
================================================================================
WHAT THE CHILD MUST LEARN
  - TENTHS: cut 1 whole into 10 equal parts; each part is 1/10, written 0.1 and
    read "zero point one" or "one tenth". 7/10 = 0.7.
  - HUNDREDTHS: cut 1 whole into 100 equal parts; each is 1/100 = 0.01.
    23/100 = 0.23. 10 hundredths make 1 tenth, and 100 hundredths make 1 whole.
  - The DECIMAL POINT separates the whole number from the parts. Place value:
    ... tens, ones . tenths, hundredths. Each place is 10 times the place on its
    right.
  - 0.1 × 10 = 1, 0.01 × 10 = 0.1 and 0.01 × 100 = 1.
  - Zero keeps places: 0.04 (4 hundredths) is not 0.4 (4 tenths).
    3.05 = 3 + 5/100.
  - Change between fraction, decimal and words: 2 35/100 = 2.35 = "two and
    thirty-five hundredths"; expanded form: 2.35 = 2 + 0.3 + 0.05.
  - Decimals on a number line: 0.7 is 7 tenths of the way from 0 to 1; 2.3 is
    between 2 and 3.
  - Money: 100 paise = ₹1, so 1 paise = ₹0.01 and 10 paise = ₹0.10. ₹12.50 = 12
    rupees 50 paise; 7 rupees 5 paise = ₹7.05.
  - Know 1/2 = 0.5, 1/4 = 0.25, 3/4 = 0.75, 1/5 = 0.2. Also 1 mm = 0.1 cm and
    1 cm = 0.01 m.

PART A — DESCRIPTIVE
--------------------
D1. A chocolate bar has 10 equal pieces. Ravi eats 3 pieces. What part of the
    bar did he eat? Write it as a fraction and as a decimal.
        [■|■|■|□|□|□|□|□|□|□]
    Answer: 3/10 = 0.3 (three tenths) of the bar.
    Teacher explains: Each piece is 1 of 10 equal parts: one tenth. 3 pieces are
    3/10. A decimal is another way to write tenths: put a 0 for no wholes, then
    the decimal point, then the number of tenths: 0.3. Read it "zero point
    three" or "three tenths". If he ate the whole bar it would be 10/10 = 1. Now
    you try: 7 pieces. (7/10 = 0.7)

D2. A square is cut into 100 small equal squares and 45 of them are shaded.
    Write the shaded part as a fraction and a decimal. Why is it also 4 tenths
    and 5 hundredths?
        ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■
        ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■
        ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■
        ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ ■
        ■ ■ ■ ■ ■ □ □ □ □ □
        □ □ □ □ □ □ □ □ □ □
        □ □ □ □ □ □ □ □ □ □
        □ □ □ □ □ □ □ □ □ □
        □ □ □ □ □ □ □ □ □ □
        □ □ □ □ □ □ □ □ □ □
    Answer: 45/100 = 0.45. The 4 full rows are 4 tenths and the 5 extra squares
    are 5 hundredths.
    Teacher explains: Each small square is 1/100 = 0.01, so 45 of them are
    45/100, written 0.45, "zero point four five" or "forty-five hundredths".
    Each row of 10 small squares is 10/100 = 1/10, one tenth. 4 full rows = 4
    tenths = 0.4, and 5 more squares = 0.05. So 0.45 = 0.4 + 0.05. The first
    digit after the point counts tenths, the second counts hundredths. Now you
    try: 62 squares shaded. (0.62 = 6 tenths + 2 hundredths)

D3. Write 23.07 in a place value chart. What is each digit worth?
        Tens | Ones | . | Tenths | Hundredths
          2  |  3   | . |   0    |     7
    Answer: 2 tens = 20, 3 ones = 3, 0 tenths, 7 hundredths = 0.07. So
    23.07 = 20 + 3 + 0.07.
    Teacher explains: Put the decimal point in its own column so that the whole
    number places are on the left and the parts on the right. Moving LEFT, each
    place is 10 times bigger; moving RIGHT, each place is 10 times smaller: 1
    one = 10 tenths, 1 tenth = 10 hundredths. The 0 in the tenths place says
    there are no tenths; without it we would write 23.7, which is a different
    number. Now you try: 40.56. (4 tens, 0 ones, 5 tenths, 6 hundredths)

D4. Meena says 0.4 and 0.04 are the same because they both have a 4. Is she
    right?
    Answer: No. 0.4 = 4/10 (4 tenths) but 0.04 = 4/100 (4 hundredths). 0.4 is 10
    times as big.
    Teacher explains: Show it on the 100-square: 0.4 is 4 whole rows (40 small
    squares); 0.04 is only 4 small squares. The zero in 0.04 is a place holder:
    it pushes the 4 into the hundredths place. In money: ₹0.40 is 40 paise but
    ₹0.04 is only 4 paise. Now you try: which is more, 0.7 or 0.07? (0.7 = 70
    hundredths)

D5. A pen costs ₹12.50. How many rupees and paise is that? Why is 50 paise
    written .50?
    Answer: 12 rupees 50 paise. 50 paise = 50/100 of a rupee = ₹0.50.
    Teacher explains: 100 paise make ₹1, so 1 paise is 1/100 of a rupee, one
    hundredth. 50 paise is 50 hundredths of a rupee: 0.50. The whole rupees go
    before the point and the paise after it, always with 2 digits. ₹12.50 is
    also ₹12.5, because 50 hundredths = 5 tenths (half a rupee). Now you try:
    ₹8.75. (8 rupees 75 paise)

D6. Arjun writes 7 rupees 5 paise as ₹7.5. What is wrong?
    Answer: ₹7.5 means 7 rupees 50 paise. 7 rupees 5 paise is ₹7.05.
    Teacher explains: Paise are hundredths of a rupee, so they need TWO places
    after the point. 5 paise = 5/100 = ₹0.05: a 0 in the tenths place, then 5 in
    the hundredths place. ₹7.5 = ₹7.50, which is 50 paise. Say it aloud: "seven
    rupees and five paise, seven point zero five". Now you try: 20 rupees 8
    paise. (₹20.08)

D7. Where are 0.7 and 2.3 on a number line?
                      A                               B
        |-|-|-|-|-|-|-|-|-|-|-|-|-|-|-|-|-|-|-|-|-|-|-|-|-|-|-|-|-|-|
        0                   1                   2                   3
        A = 0.7, B = 2.3
    Answer: 0.7 is at the 7th mark after 0 when 0 to 1 is cut into tenths. 2.3
    is at the 3rd mark after 2.
    Teacher explains: Cut the space between two whole numbers into 10 equal
    steps; each step is 0.1. Count from 0: 0.1, 0.2, ..., 0.7. Notice that after
    0.9 comes 1.0 = 1, not 0.10. For 2.3, start at 2 and count 3 tenths: 2.1,
    2.2, 2.3. 2.5 is halfway between 2 and 3. Now you try: what is at the 6th
    mark after 1? (1.6)

D8. Ten 10-paise coins are put together. How much money is that? What is
    0.1 × 10?
    Answer: ₹1. 0.1 × 10 = 1.
    Teacher explains: 10 paise = ₹0.10 = ₹0.1, one tenth of a rupee. Ten of them
    make 10 tenths = 1 whole rupee. So 0.1 × 10 = 1. In the same way, a 1-paise
    coin is ₹0.01; ten of them make ₹0.10, so 0.01 × 10 = 0.1, and a hundred of
    them make ₹1, so 0.01 × 100 = 1. When a number is multiplied by 10, each
    digit moves one place to the left. Now you try: 0.1 × 7. (0.7)

D9. Write 5.38 in expanded form, with decimals and with fractions.
    Answer: 5.38 = 5 + 0.3 + 0.08 = 5 + 3/10 + 8/100.
    Teacher explains: Take each digit with its place value: 5 ones = 5, 3 tenths
    = 0.3 = 3/10, 8 hundredths = 0.08 = 8/100. Add them back to check:
    5 + 0.3 + 0.08 = 5.38. A common mistake is to write 0.8 for the last part;
    the 8 is in the SECOND place after the point, so it is hundredths. Now you
    try: 12.46. (10 + 2 + 0.4 + 0.06)

D10. On Monday 2.6 cm of rain fell, and on Tuesday 0.4 cm. How much rain fell in
     the two days?
            2.6
          + 0.4
          -----
            3.0
     Answer: 3 cm. 2.6 + 0.4 = 3.0 = 3.
     Teacher explains: Add tenths to tenths: 6 tenths + 4 tenths = 10 tenths,
     and 10 tenths make 1 whole. So 2 + 1 = 3, and there are 0 tenths left:
     3.0 = 3 cm. Line up the decimal points when you write it in columns. Adding
     a decimal to a whole number is easy: 3 + 0.7 = 3.7. Now you try: 4.8 + 0.2
     and 5 + 0.35. (5; 5.35)

D11. Write 1/2, 1/4, 3/4 and 1/5 as decimals.
     Answer: 1/2 = 0.5, 1/4 = 0.25, 3/4 = 0.75, 1/5 = 0.2.
     Teacher explains: Make an equivalent fraction with denominator 10 or 100,
     then write it as a decimal. 1/2 = 5/10 = 0.5. 1/4 = 25/100 = 0.25 (multiply
     top and bottom by 25). 3/4 = 75/100 = 0.75. 1/5 = 2/10 = 0.2. Think of
     money: half a rupee is 50 paise, a quarter of a rupee is 25 paise. Now you
     try: 2/5 and 3/20. (0.4; 0.15)

D12. A pencil is 12 cm 7 mm long. Write its length in centimetres using a
     decimal. A ribbon is 35 cm long; write its length in metres.
     Answer: 12 cm 7 mm = 12.7 cm. 35 cm = 0.35 m.
     Teacher explains: 1 cm = 10 mm, so 1 mm is one tenth of a centimetre: 1 mm
     = 0.1 cm and 7 mm = 0.7 cm. So 12 cm 7 mm = 12.7 cm. 1 m = 100 cm, so 1 cm
     is one hundredth of a metre: 1 cm = 0.01 m and 35 cm = 0.35 m. Look at the
     ruler: the small marks between centimetres are tenths. Now you try: 3 m
     5 cm in metres. (3.05 m)

PART B — MATHEMATICAL ONLY
--------------------------
M1. Write as a decimal. (Tenths.)
    a) 1/10      b) 3/10      c) 7/10      d) 9/10      e) 10/10
    f) 0/10      g) 5/10      h) 2/10      i) 4/10      j) 6/10
    k) 8/10      l) 3 7/10    m) 12 1/10   n) 5 9/10    o) 40 3/10
    p) 1 5/10    q) 23/10     r) 58/10     s) 100/10    t) 11/10
    Answers: a) 0.1   b) 0.3   c) 0.7   d) 0.9   e) 1   f) 0   g) 0.5   h) 0.2
             i) 0.4   j) 0.6   k) 0.8   l) 3.7   m) 12.1   n) 5.9   o) 40.3
             p) 1.5   q) 2.3   r) 5.8   s) 10   t) 1.1

M2. Write as a decimal. (Hundredths.)
    a) 1/100      b) 5/100      c) 9/100      d) 10/100     e) 23/100
    f) 45/100     g) 50/100     h) 99/100     i) 100/100    j) 7/100
    k) 60/100     l) 75/100     m) 2 35/100   n) 4 8/100    o) 10 1/100
    p) 250/100    q) 307/100    r) 1 90/100   s) 88/100     t) 3 3/100
    Answers: a) 0.01   b) 0.05   c) 0.09   d) 0.10   e) 0.23   f) 0.45   g) 0.50
             h) 0.99   i) 1.00   j) 0.07   k) 0.60   l) 0.75   m) 2.35   n) 4.08
             o) 10.01   p) 2.50   q) 3.07   r) 1.90   s) 0.88   t) 3.03

M3. Write as a fraction with denominator 10 or 100 (use a mixed number when
    there are wholes).
    a) 0.9     b) 0.09    c) 0.4     d) 0.04    e) 0.40
    f) 2.35    g) 1.7     h) 6.05    i) 0.71    j) 0.1
    k) 0.01    l) 12.6    m) 3.33    n) 0.5     o) 8.02
    p) 0.99    q) 4.1     r) 20.25   s) 0.3     t) 7.07
    Answers: a) 9/10   b) 9/100   c) 4/10   d) 4/100   e) 40/100   f) 2 35/100
             g) 1 7/10   h) 6 5/100   i) 71/100   j) 1/10   k) 1/100
             l) 12 6/10   m) 3 33/100   n) 5/10   o) 8 2/100   p) 99/100
             q) 4 1/10   r) 20 25/100   s) 3/10   t) 7 7/100

M4. Write a) to h) in words (as tenths or hundredths). Write i) to p) as
    decimals.
    a) 0.6                         b) 0.25
    c) 0.07                        d) 3.4
    e) 2.09                        f) 0.5
    g) 11.3                        h) 0.81
    i) three and four tenths       j) twenty-five hundredths
    k) seven hundredths            l) two and nine hundredths
    m) nine tenths                 n) fifteen and six tenths
    o) one hundredth               p) four and sixty hundredths
    Answers:
      a) six tenths
      b) twenty-five hundredths
      c) seven hundredths
      d) three and four tenths
      e) two and nine hundredths
      f) five tenths
      g) eleven and three tenths
      h) eighty-one hundredths
      i) 3.4

      k) 0.07
      l) 2.09

      p) 4.60

M5. What is the digit worth? Give its place and its value.
    a) the 5 in 34.56   b) the 6 in 34.56   c) the 3 in 34.56
    d) the 7 in 0.7     e) the 7 in 0.07    f) the 2 in 12.9
    g) the 9 in 12.9    h) the 8 in 8.41    i) the 1 in 8.41
    j) the 4 in 8.41    k) the 3 in 305.2   l) the 5 in 305.2
    m) the 6 in 1.06    n) the 9 in 90.5    o) the 2 in 0.25
    p) the 1 in 71.38
    Answers: a) 5 tenths = 0.5   b) 6 hundredths = 0.06   c) 3 tens = 30
             d) 7 tenths = 0.7   e) 7 hundredths = 0.07   f) 2 ones = 2
             g) 9 tenths = 0.9   h) 8 ones = 8   i) 1 hundredth = 0.01
             j) 4 tenths = 0.4   k) 3 hundreds = 300   l) 5 ones = 5
             m) 6 hundredths = 0.06   n) 9 tens = 90   o) 2 tenths = 0.2
             p) 1 one = 1

M6. Expanded form. Write a) to h) as a sum of place values; write i) to p) as
    one decimal.
    a) 4.37 = __                b) 5.08 = __
    c) 40.6 = __                d) 0.72 = __
    e) 12.45 = __               f) 9.9 = __
    g) 3.01 = __                h) 60.3 = __
    i) 6 + 0.2 + 0.05 = __      j) 20 + 3 + 0.9 = __
    k) 0.08 + 5 = __            l) 3 + 7/10 + 2/100 = __
    m) 10 + 0.01 = __           n) 0.4 + 0.03 = __
    o) 7 + 6/100 = __           p) 50 + 1/10 + 1/100 = __
    Answers: a) 4 + 0.3 + 0.07   b) 5 + 0.08   c) 40 + 0.6   d) 0.7 + 0.02
             e) 10 + 2 + 0.4 + 0.05   f) 9 + 0.9   g) 3 + 0.01   h) 60 + 0.3
             i) 6.25   j) 23.9   k) 5.08   l) 3.72   m) 10.01   n) 0.43
             o) 7.06   p) 50.11

M7. Write the decimal at each letter.
    Line 1:
          D           A           B                 C
    |-----|-----|-----|-----|-----|-----|-----|-----|-----|-----|
    0                                                           1

    Line 2:
                E              G     F                 H

    a) A   b) B   c) C   d) D   e) E   f) F
    g) G   h) H   i) I   j) J   k) K   l) L
    Answers: a) 0.3   b) 0.5   c) 0.8   d) 0.1   e) 2.4   f) 3.1   g) 2.9
             h) 3.7   i) 0.32   j) 0.35   k) 0.39   l) 0.31

M8. Money (100 paise = ₹1). Write a) to j) in rupees with a decimal point. Write
    k) to t) in rupees and paise.
    a) 12 rupees 50 paise   b) 7 rupees 5 paise     c) 75 paise
    d) 350 paise            e) 5 paise              f) 100 paise

    j) 40 paise             k) ₹3.45                l) ₹0.60

M9. Tenths and hundredths. Work out or fill in.
    a) 10 × 0.1              b) 0.1 × 10              c) 5 × 0.1
    d) 20 × 0.1              e) 10 × 0.01             f) 100 × 0.01
    g) 30 × 0.01             h) 0.1 + 0.1 + 0.1       i) 0.9 + 0.1
    j) 0.09 + 0.01           k) 0.99 + 0.01           l) 1 − 0.1
    m) 1 − 0.01              n) 0.3 × 10              o) 1 = __ tenths
    p) 1 = __ hundredths     q) 0.1 = __ hundredths   r) 2.5 = __ tenths
    s) 0.4 = __ hundredths   t) 3 = __ tenths
    Answers: a) 1   b) 1   c) 0.5   d) 2   e) 0.1   f) 1   g) 0.3   h) 0.3
             i) 1   j) 0.1   k) 1   l) 0.9   m) 0.99   n) 3   o) 10   p) 100
             q) 10   r) 25   s) 40   t) 30

M10. Add. Think in tenths and hundredths (10 tenths make 1).
     a) 3 + 0.4          b) 2.6 + 0.4        c) 5.3 + 0.7
     d) 1.25 + 0.75      e) 0.5 + 0.5        f) 7 + 0.09
     g) 4.2 + 0.3        h) 0.8 + 0.3        i) 9.9 + 0.1
     j) 6.45 + 0.05      k) 2 + 0.6 + 0.03   l) 10 + 0.1
     m) 3.7 + 0.5        n) 1.5 + 1.5        o) 0.25 + 0.25
     p) 4.8 + 0.2        q) 12.3 + 0.07      r) 0.6 + 0.6
     s) 5.95 + 0.05      t) 8 + 0.8
     Answers: a) 3.4   b) 3   c) 6   d) 2   e) 1   f) 7.09   g) 4.5   h) 1.1
              i) 10   j) 6.5   k) 2.63   l) 10.1   m) 4.2   n) 3   o) 0.5   p) 5
              q) 12.37   r) 1.2   s) 6   t) 8.8

M11. Make the denominator 10 or 100, then write as a decimal.
     a) 1/2     b) 1/4     c) 3/4     d) 1/5     e) 2/5
     f) 3/5     g) 4/5     h) 1/20    i) 7/20    j) 1/25
     k) 3/50    l) 11/20   m) 9/25    n) 13/50   o) 1/50
     p) 2 1/2   q) 3 3/4   r) 1 1/5   s) 4 1/4
     Answers:
       a) 1/2 = 5/10 = 0.5
       b) 1/4 = 25/100 = 0.25
       c) 3/4 = 75/100 = 0.75
       d) 1/5 = 2/10 = 0.2
       e) 2/5 = 4/10 = 0.4
       f) 3/5 = 6/10 = 0.6
       g) 4/5 = 8/10 = 0.8
       h) 1/20 = 5/100 = 0.05
       i) 7/20 = 35/100 = 0.35
       j) 1/25 = 4/100 = 0.04
       k) 3/50 = 6/100 = 0.06
       l) 11/20 = 55/100 = 0.55
       m) 9/25 = 36/100 = 0.36
       n) 13/50 = 26/100 = 0.26
       o) 1/50 = 2/100 = 0.02
       p) 2 5/10 = 2.5
       q) 3 75/100 = 3.75
       r) 1 2/10 = 1.2
       s) 4 25/100 = 4.25

M12. Lengths as decimals. (1 cm = 10 mm, 1 m = 100 cm.)
     a) 12 cm 7 mm = __ cm   b) 3 m 45 cm = __ m     c) 5 mm = __ cm
     d) 8 cm = __ m          e) 1 m 5 cm = __ m      f) 2.6 cm = __ mm
     g) 0.35 m = __ cm       h) 6.07 m = __ cm       i) 90 cm = __ m
     j) 15 mm = __ cm        k) 7 m 70 cm = __ m     l) 0.1 cm = __ mm
     m) 1.5 m = __ cm        n) 3 mm = __ cm         o) 250 cm = __ m
     p) 10 cm 1 mm = __ cm
     Answers: a) 12.7   b) 3.45   c) 0.5   d) 0.08   e) 1.05   f) 26   g) 35
              h) 607   i) 0.9   j) 1.5   k) 7.7   l) 1   m) 150   n) 0.3
              o) 2.5   p) 10.1

TEACHING TIPS
  Struggling: build tenths with a paper strip cut into 10 and hundredths with a
  10 by 10 grid; use real coins and a price list; always say "tenths" and
  "hundredths" rather than "point four five" until the meaning is secure; keep a
  place value chart with a column for the point. Harder: numbers such as 305.06
  and 0.99, thousandths as a preview (0.001), shopping bills with rupees and
  paise, and converting measurements both ways (4.05 m = 405 cm).


================================================================================
TOPIC 4.26 — COMPARING DECIMALS
================================================================================
WHAT THE CHILD MUST LEARN
  - To compare decimals, line up the decimal points. Compare the whole numbers
    first; if they are equal, compare the tenths; if those are equal, compare
    the hundredths.
  - Zeros at the END of the decimal part do not change the value: 0.3 = 0.30 (3
    tenths = 30 hundredths). Writing both numbers with the same number of
    decimal places makes comparing easy.
  - More digits does NOT mean bigger: 0.5 > 0.45, because 0.50 is 50 hundredths
    and 0.45 is only 45 hundredths.
  - 1.09 < 1.1, because 9 hundredths is less than 1 tenth (10 hundredths). Zeros
    in the MIDDLE matter: 4.05 is not 4.5.
  - Order sets of decimals, smallest first or largest first, and place them on a
    number line.
  - Between any two decimals there are more decimals: between 0.3 and 0.4 lie
    0.31, 0.32, ..., 0.39; halfway is 0.35.
  - Round to the nearest whole number by looking at the tenths digit (5 or more:
    round up): 3.6 → 4, 3.4 → 3, 3.5 → 4. Round to the nearest tenth by looking
    at the hundredths digit: 2.47 → 2.5.
  - Round money to the nearest rupee: ₹47.65 → ₹48, ₹12.49 → ₹12.

PART A — DESCRIPTIVE
--------------------
D1. In a 100 m race Kavya ran it in 12.5 seconds and Riya in 12.45 seconds. Who
    was faster?
          12.50
          12.45
          -- same whole number, compare tenths: 5 > 4
    Answer: Riya. 12.45 < 12.50, so Riya took less time.
    Teacher explains: Line up the decimal points and write both with two decimal
    places: 12.50 and 12.45. The whole numbers are the same (12). Tenths: 5 and
    4, and 4 is less. So 12.45 < 12.5. In a race, the SMALLER time wins, so Riya
    was faster, by 0.05 seconds. Now you try: 9.8 s or 9.75 s, which is faster?
    (9.75 s)

D2. Ravi says 0.45 is bigger than 0.5 because 45 is bigger than 5. Explain his
    mistake.
    Answer: 0.5 is bigger. 0.5 = 0.50 = 50 hundredths, and 0.45 is 45
    hundredths. So 0.5 > 0.45.
    Teacher explains: Ravi is reading the digits after the point as whole
    numbers. The places matter: in 0.5 the 5 is in the TENTHS place; in 0.45 the
    4 is in the tenths place. Compare tenths first: 5 tenths > 4 tenths, so 0.5
    is bigger. Money makes it clear: ₹0.50 (50 paise) is more than ₹0.45 (45
    paise). A trick: add a zero so both have two places, 0.50 and 0.45. Now you
    try: 0.7 or 0.65? (0.7 > 0.65)

D3. Are 0.3 and 0.30 the same or different?
    Answer: The same. 0.3 = 3 tenths = 30 hundredths = 0.30.
    Teacher explains: Shade 3 columns of a 100-square: that is 3 tenths, and it
    is also 30 small squares, 30 hundredths. Adding a zero at the END of the
    decimal part does not change the value, just as ₹0.3 and ₹0.30 are both 30
    paise. But a zero straight after the point does change it: 0.03 is only 3
    hundredths. Now you try: is 2.5 the same as 2.50? Is 2.5 the same as 2.05?
    (yes; no)

D4. Which is bigger: 1.09 or 1.1?
    Answer: 1.1 is bigger. 1.1 = 1.10, and 1.10 > 1.09.
    Teacher explains: Wholes: both 1. Tenths: 1.09 has 0 tenths and 1.1 has 1
    tenth, so 1.1 is already bigger; we do not even need the hundredths. Another
    way: 1.09 is 1 and 9 hundredths, 1.1 is 1 and 10 hundredths. On a number
    line 1.09 is just before 1.1. Children often think 1.09 is bigger because
    9 > 1 or because it is longer. Now you try: 3.2 or 3.19? (3.2 > 3.19)

D5. Compare 23.47 and 23.5 using a place value chart.
        Tens | Ones | . | Tenths | Hundredths
          2  |  3   | . |   4    |     7
          2  |  3   | . |   5    |     0
    Answer: 23.47 < 23.5.
    Teacher explains: Write the numbers one under the other with the points in a
    line. Tens: 2 and 2, same. Ones: 3 and 3, same. Tenths: 4 and 5, and 4 < 5,
    so 23.47 < 23.5. Stop at the first place where the digits are different; the
    later places do not matter. Now you try: 45.08 or 45.1? (45.08 < 45.1)

D6. The heights of four children are 1.35 m, 1.4 m, 1.28 m and 1.3 m. Put them
    in order from shortest to tallest.
    Answer: 1.28 m < 1.3 m < 1.35 m < 1.4 m.
    Teacher explains: Write all with two decimal places: 1.35, 1.40, 1.28, 1.30.
    All have 1 whole metre. Compare the hundredths as whole numbers now:
    28 < 30 < 35 < 40. So the order is 1.28, 1.3, 1.35, 1.4. In centimetres:
    128 cm, 130 cm, 135 cm, 140 cm. Now you try: order 0.6, 0.06, 0.66, 0.16.
    (0.06 < 0.16 < 0.6 < 0.66)

D7. Show 0.2, 0.25 and 0.3 on a number line. Name three decimals between 0.2 and
    0.3.
                                 ▲
        |----|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|
        0.2                     0.25                     0.3
    Answer: 0.25 is halfway between 0.2 and 0.3. Three decimals between them are
    0.21, 0.24, 0.28 (any of 0.21 to 0.29).
    Teacher explains: Zoom in on the part of the line from 0.2 to 0.3 and cut it
    into 10 equal steps: each step is 0.01. The marks are 0.21, 0.22, ..., 0.29.
    0.2 = 0.20 and 0.3 = 0.30, so the numbers between have 2 in the tenths
    place. The 5th mark is 0.25, halfway. Now you try: name two decimals between
    1.6 and 1.7. (for example 1.62 and 1.65)

D8. How many decimals with two places are there between 0.7 and 0.8? Which one
    is exactly halfway?
    Answer: Nine: 0.71, 0.72, 0.73, 0.74, 0.75, 0.76, 0.77, 0.78, 0.79. 0.75 is
    halfway.
    Teacher explains: 0.7 = 0.70 and 0.8 = 0.80. Between 70 hundredths and 80
    hundredths lie 71, 72, ..., 79 hundredths: nine numbers. Halfway between 70
    and 80 is 75, so 0.75 is halfway. And between 0.75 and 0.76 there are even
    more decimals (with three places), so there is no "next" decimal after 0.75.
    Now you try: halfway between 2.4 and 2.5? (2.45)

D9. A bag of rice weighs 3.6 kg. What is that to the nearest kilogram? What
    about 3.4 kg and 3.5 kg?
                        a   b   c
        |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
        3                  3.5                  4
        a = 3.4 (→ 3), b = 3.5 (→ 4), c = 3.6 (→ 4)
    Answer: 3.6 kg → 4 kg. 3.4 kg → 3 kg. 3.5 kg → 4 kg.
    Teacher explains: 3.6 lies between 3 and 4. On the number line, 3.6 is past
    the halfway mark 3.5, so it is nearer to 4. 3.4 is before halfway, so it is
    nearer to 3. 3.5 is exactly halfway; the rule is to round UP. To round to
    the nearest whole number, look only at the tenths digit: 0 to 4 round down,
    5 to 9 round up. Now you try: 7.49 and 12.5. (7; 13)

D10. Amma's vegetable bill is ₹47.65 and the milk bill is ₹12.49. Round each to
     the nearest rupee.
     Answer: ₹47.65 → ₹48. ₹12.49 → ₹12.
     Teacher explains: The paise decide. 65 paise is 50 paise or more, so round
     up to the next rupee: ₹48. 49 paise is less than 50 paise, so round down:
     ₹12. Rounding helps us estimate a total quickly: about ₹48 + ₹12 = ₹60 (the
     exact total is ₹60.14). Now you try: ₹99.50 and ₹5.05. (₹100; ₹5)

D11. Round 2.47, 6.93 and 3.96 to the nearest tenth.
     Answer: 2.47 → 2.5. 6.93 → 6.9. 3.96 → 4.0.
     Teacher explains: To the nearest tenth means one decimal place. Look at the
     HUNDREDTHS digit. 2.47: the 7 is 5 or more, so the tenths go up from 4 to
     5: 2.5. 6.93: the 3 is less than 5, so keep 6.9. 3.96: the 6 makes the 9
     tenths go up to 10 tenths, which is 1 whole: 3.9 becomes 4.0. We write 4.0
     to show it is rounded to tenths. Now you try: 5.55 and 0.04. (5.6; 0.0)

D12. In the long jump Arjun jumped 3.8 m, Sonu 3.75 m, Imran 3.09 m and Dev
     3.9 m. Who came first, second, third and fourth? Sonu says he came second
     because 75 is the biggest number. Is he right?
     Answer: 1st Dev (3.9 m), 2nd Arjun (3.8 m), 3rd Sonu (3.75 m), 4th Imran
     (3.09 m). Sonu is wrong: 3.75 < 3.8.
     Teacher explains: Write with two places: 3.80, 3.75, 3.09, 3.90. Compare
     tenths: 9, 8, 7, 0. So 3.9 > 3.8 > 3.75 > 3.09. Sonu compared 75 with 8 and
     9 as whole numbers, but the 7 in 3.75 is in the tenths place, and 7 tenths
     is less than 8 tenths. Now you try: order 0.9, 0.89, 0.98, 0.8, largest
     first. (0.98 > 0.9 > 0.89 > 0.8)

PART B — MATHEMATICAL ONLY
--------------------------
M1. Write <, > or =. (Tenths.)
    a) 0.3 __ 0.7     b) 2.4 __ 4.2     c) 5 __ 5.0       d) 0.9 __ 0.1
    e) 3.6 __ 3.9     f) 7.1 __ 6.9     g) 0.5 __ 0.5     h) 10.2 __ 9.8
    i) 1.0 __ 1       j) 4.4 __ 4.3     k) 0.8 __ 1.2     l) 12.5 __ 12.5
    m) 0 __ 0.1       n) 8.9 __ 9       o) 2.7 __ 7.2     p) 6.6 __ 6.0
    q) 15.3 __ 5.3    r) 0.4 __ 0.6     s) 9.9 __ 10      t) 3.1 __ 1.3
    Answers: a) 0.3 < 0.7   b) 2.4 < 4.2   c) 5 = 5.0   d) 0.9 > 0.1
             e) 3.6 < 3.9   f) 7.1 > 6.9   g) 0.5 = 0.5   h) 10.2 > 9.8
             i) 1.0 = 1   j) 4.4 > 4.3   k) 0.8 < 1.2   l) 12.5 = 12.5
             m) 0 < 0.1   n) 8.9 < 9   o) 2.7 < 7.2   p) 6.6 > 6.0
             q) 15.3 > 5.3   r) 0.4 < 0.6   s) 9.9 < 10   t) 3.1 > 1.3

M2. Write <, > or =. (Hundredths.)
    a) 0.45 __ 0.54     b) 0.23 __ 0.32     c) 1.25 __ 1.52
    d) 0.09 __ 0.10     e) 3.47 __ 3.74     f) 0.66 __ 0.66
    g) 2.08 __ 2.80     h) 5.99 __ 6.01     i) 0.15 __ 0.51
    j) 4.50 __ 4.05     k) 7.31 __ 7.13     l) 0.01 __ 0.10
    m) 9.09 __ 9.90     n) 12.34 __ 12.43   o) 0.70 __ 0.07
    p) 6.12 __ 6.21     q) 1.00 __ 0.99     r) 3.33 __ 3.30
    s) 8.48 __ 8.84     t) 0.58 __ 0.85
    Answers: a) 0.45 < 0.54   b) 0.23 < 0.32   c) 1.25 < 1.52   d) 0.09 < 0.10
             e) 3.47 < 3.74   f) 0.66 = 0.66   g) 2.08 < 2.80   h) 5.99 < 6.01
             i) 0.15 < 0.51   j) 4.50 > 4.05   k) 7.31 > 7.13   l) 0.01 < 0.10
             m) 9.09 < 9.90   n) 12.34 < 12.43   o) 0.70 > 0.07   p) 6.12 < 6.21
             q) 1.00 > 0.99   r) 3.33 > 3.30   s) 8.48 < 8.84   t) 0.58 < 0.85

M3. Write <, > or =. Careful: these have different numbers of decimal places.
    a) 0.5 __ 0.45     b) 0.3 __ 0.30     c) 1.09 __ 1.1     d) 0.8 __ 0.08
    e) 2.1 __ 2.09     f) 0.6 __ 0.59     g) 3 __ 2.99       h) 0.07 __ 0.7
    i) 4.5 __ 4.49     j) 0.2 __ 0.19     k) 5.05 __ 5.5     l) 0.9 __ 0.90
    m) 1.4 __ 1.36     n) 0.1 __ 0.01     o) 6.7 __ 6.71     p) 2.35 __ 2.4
    q) 0.99 __ 1       r) 7.08 __ 7.8     s) 0.4 __ 0.40     t) 10.1 __ 10.09
    u) 0.25 __ 0.3     v) 3.9 __ 3.89     w) 8.01 __ 8.1     x) 0.6 __ 0.06
    Answers: a) 0.5 > 0.45   b) 0.3 = 0.30   c) 1.09 < 1.1   d) 0.8 > 0.08
             e) 2.1 > 2.09   f) 0.6 > 0.59   g) 3 > 2.99   h) 0.07 < 0.7
             i) 4.5 > 4.49   j) 0.2 > 0.19   k) 5.05 < 5.5   l) 0.9 = 0.90
             m) 1.4 > 1.36   n) 0.1 > 0.01   o) 6.7 < 6.71   p) 2.35 < 2.4
             q) 0.99 < 1   r) 7.08 < 7.8   s) 0.4 = 0.40   t) 10.1 > 10.09
             u) 0.25 < 0.3   v) 3.9 > 3.89   w) 8.01 < 8.1   x) 0.6 > 0.06

M4. Write both numbers with two decimal places, then write <, > or =.
    a) 0.4 __ 0.35   b) 3.1 __ 3.08   c) 5 __ 4.95     d) 0.9 __ 0.91
    e) 2.5 __ 2.45   f) 7 __ 7.05     g) 0.1 __ 0.11   h) 6.3 __ 6.29
    i) 1.2 __ 1.25   j) 0.7 __ 0.65   k) 9 __ 8.99     l) 4.6 __ 4.06
    m) 0.25 __ 0.2   n) 1.5 __ 1.49   o) 8.4 __ 8.44   p) 0.08 __ 0.8
    Answers: a) 0.40 > 0.35   b) 3.10 > 3.08   c) 5.00 > 4.95   d) 0.90 < 0.91
             e) 2.50 > 2.45   f) 7.00 < 7.05   g) 0.10 < 0.11   h) 6.30 > 6.29
             i) 1.20 < 1.25   j) 0.70 > 0.65   k) 9.00 > 8.99   l) 4.60 > 4.06
             m) 0.25 > 0.20   n) 1.50 > 1.49   o) 8.40 < 8.44   p) 0.08 < 0.80

M5. Find the largest and the smallest number in each set.
    a) 0.8, 0.08, 0.18, 0.81   b) 2.5, 2.05, 2.55, 2.15
    c) 0.3, 0.29, 0.31, 0.03   d) 1.1, 1.01, 1.11, 1.02
    e) 4.9, 4.09, 4.99, 5      f) 0.6, 0.66, 0.06, 0.65
    g) 7.2, 7.12, 7.21, 7.02   h) 3.45, 3.5, 3.4, 3.54
    i) 9.9, 10.1, 9.99, 10     j) 0.5, 0.45, 0.55, 0.4
    Answers:
      a) largest 0.81, smallest 0.08
      b) largest 2.55, smallest 2.05
      c) largest 0.31, smallest 0.03
      d) largest 1.11, smallest 1.01
      e) largest 5, smallest 4.09
      f) largest 0.66, smallest 0.06
      g) largest 7.21, smallest 7.02
      h) largest 3.54, smallest 3.4
      i) largest 10.1, smallest 9.9
      j) largest 0.55, smallest 0.4

M6. Put in order, smallest first.
    a) 0.5, 0.45, 0.3, 0.54        b) 1.2, 1.02, 1.22, 1.12
    c) 0.7, 0.07, 0.77, 0.17       d) 3.09, 3.9, 3.19, 3.1
    e) 2.5, 2.45, 2.54, 2.4        f) 0.1, 0.01, 0.11, 0.2
    g) 6, 5.99, 6.01, 5.9          h) 4.8, 4.08, 4.88, 4.18
    i) 0.36, 0.6, 0.63, 0.3        j) 10.5, 10.05, 10.55, 10.15
    Answers:
      a) 0.3 < 0.45 < 0.5 < 0.54
      b) 1.02 < 1.12 < 1.2 < 1.22
      c) 0.07 < 0.17 < 0.7 < 0.77
      d) 3.09 < 3.1 < 3.19 < 3.9
      e) 2.4 < 2.45 < 2.5 < 2.54
      f) 0.01 < 0.1 < 0.11 < 0.2
      g) 5.9 < 5.99 < 6 < 6.01
      h) 4.08 < 4.18 < 4.8 < 4.88
      i) 0.3 < 0.36 < 0.6 < 0.63
      j) 10.05 < 10.15 < 10.5 < 10.55

M7. Put in order, largest first.
    a) 0.9, 0.89, 0.98, 0.8        b) 1.35, 1.4, 1.28, 1.3
    c) 5.5, 5.05, 5.55, 5.15       d) 0.6, 0.06, 0.66, 0.16
    e) 2.01, 2.1, 2.11, 2          f) 7.7, 7.07, 7.77, 7.17
    g) 0.25, 0.5, 0.2, 0.52        h) 12.4, 12.04, 12.44, 12.14
    Answers:
      a) 0.98 > 0.9 > 0.89 > 0.8
      b) 1.4 > 1.35 > 1.3 > 1.28
      c) 5.55 > 5.5 > 5.15 > 5.05
      d) 0.66 > 0.6 > 0.16 > 0.06
      e) 2.11 > 2.1 > 2.01 > 2
      f) 7.77 > 7.7 > 7.17 > 7.07
      g) 0.52 > 0.5 > 0.25 > 0.2
      h) 12.44 > 12.4 > 12.14 > 12.04

M8. Many decimals lie between two numbers. Find the one exactly halfway and
    write it like this: 0.3 < 0.35 < 0.4.
    a) 0.3 and 0.4   b) 2 and 2.1     c) 0.9 and 1     d) 1.5 and 1.6
    e) 4 and 5       f) 0.7 and 0.8   g) 3.2 and 3.3   h) 0.1 and 0.2
    i) 6.9 and 7     j) 9.4 and 9.5   k) 0.2 and 0.3   l) 0 and 0.1
    m) 0.5 and 0.6   n) 12 and 13
    Answers: a) 0.3 < 0.35 < 0.4   b) 2 < 2.05 < 2.1   c) 0.9 < 0.95 < 1
             d) 1.5 < 1.55 < 1.6   e) 4 < 4.5 < 5   f) 0.7 < 0.75 < 0.8
             g) 3.2 < 3.25 < 3.3   h) 0.1 < 0.15 < 0.2   i) 6.9 < 6.95 < 7
             j) 9.4 < 9.45 < 9.5   k) 0.2 < 0.25 < 0.3   l) 0 < 0.05 < 0.1
             m) 0.5 < 0.55 < 0.6   n) 12 < 12.5 < 13

M9. Round to the nearest whole number.
    a) 3.6     b) 3.4     c) 3.5     d) 7.2     e) 7.8
    f) 0.5     g) 0.4     h) 9.5     i) 2.49    j) 12.05
    k) 12.51   l) 15.09   m) 0.95    n) 19.7    o) 4.45
    p) 6.99    q) 99.5    r) 1.01    s) 8.5     t) 25.25
    Answers: a) 3.6 → 4   b) 3.4 → 3   c) 3.5 → 4   d) 7.2 → 7   e) 7.8 → 8
             f) 0.5 → 1   g) 0.4 → 0   h) 9.5 → 10   i) 2.49 → 2   j) 12.05 → 12
             k) 12.51 → 13   l) 15.09 → 15   m) 0.95 → 1   n) 19.7 → 20
             o) 4.45 → 4   p) 6.99 → 7   q) 99.5 → 100   r) 1.01 → 1
             s) 8.5 → 9   t) 25.25 → 25

M10. Round to the nearest tenth (one decimal place).
     a) 2.47    b) 6.93    c) 3.96    d) 0.05    e) 7.04
     f) 1.25    g) 4.81    h) 0.99    i) 5.55    j) 8.12
     k) 0.34    l) 9.95    m) 12.68   n) 3.45    o) 0.72
     p) 6.06    q) 2.01    r) 15.55   s) 0.15    t) 4.44
     Answers: a) 2.47 → 2.5   b) 6.93 → 6.9   c) 3.96 → 4.0   d) 0.05 → 0.1
              e) 7.04 → 7.0   f) 1.25 → 1.3   g) 4.81 → 4.8   h) 0.99 → 1.0
              i) 5.55 → 5.6   j) 8.12 → 8.1   k) 0.34 → 0.3   l) 9.95 → 10.0
              m) 12.68 → 12.7   n) 3.45 → 3.5   o) 0.72 → 0.7   p) 6.06 → 6.1
              q) 2.01 → 2.0   r) 15.55 → 15.6   s) 0.15 → 0.2   t) 4.44 → 4.4

M11. Round to the nearest rupee.
     a) ₹23.50    b) ₹47.65    c) ₹12.49    d) ₹0.75
     e) ₹99.50    f) ₹5.05     g) ₹8.99     h) ₹150.40
     i) ₹19.60    j) ₹0.35     k) ₹64.50    l) ₹30.30
     m) ₹7.51     n) ₹245.95   o) ₹11.11    p) ₹999.50
     Answers: a) ₹23.50 → ₹24   b) ₹47.65 → ₹48   c) ₹12.49 → ₹12
              d) ₹0.75 → ₹1   e) ₹99.50 → ₹100   f) ₹5.05 → ₹5   g) ₹8.99 → ₹9
              h) ₹150.40 → ₹150   i) ₹19.60 → ₹20   j) ₹0.35 → ₹0
              k) ₹64.50 → ₹65   l) ₹30.30 → ₹30   m) ₹7.51 → ₹8
              n) ₹245.95 → ₹246   o) ₹11.11 → ₹11   p) ₹999.50 → ₹1000

M12. True or false?
     a) 0.5 > 0.45    b) 0.3 = 0.30    c) 1.09 > 1.1    d) 0.8 < 0.08
     e) 2.5 = 2.50    f) 0.25 = 1/4    g) 3/4 > 0.7     h) 0.6 < 3/5
     i) 1/2 = 0.5     j) 0.09 > 0.1    k) 4.05 = 4.5    l) 0.99 < 1
     m) 7.1 > 7.09    n) 2/10 = 0.02   o) 0.4 > 1/4     p) 5.6 < 5.60
     Answers: a) true   b) true   c) false   d) false   e) true   f) true
              g) true   h) false   i) true   j) false   k) false   l) true
              m) true   n) false   o) true   p) false

TEACHING TIPS
  Struggling: always rewrite both numbers with the same number of decimal places
  and read them as hundredths ("50 hundredths and 45 hundredths"); use money
  (paise) and a metre stick marked in centimetres; draw the number line between
  two whole numbers before rounding. Harder: order sets that mix fractions and
  decimals (3/4, 0.7, 0.8, 4/5), find decimals between 0.35 and 0.36, round to
  the nearest 10 and to the nearest tenth, and make the largest and smallest
  decimals from digit cards such as 4, 0, 7.


================================================================================
TOPIC 4.27 — ROMAN NUMERALS
================================================================================
WHAT THE CHILD MUST LEARN
  - The seven Roman symbols: I = 1, V = 5, X = 10, L = 50, C = 100, D = 500,
    M = 1000. There is no symbol for zero.
  - ADD when a symbol is followed by a smaller or equal one: VI = 5 + 1 = 6,
    XV = 15, LX = 60, MDC = 1600.
  - I, X, C and M can be repeated, but at most three times in a row (III = 3,
    XXX = 30, CCC = 300, MMM = 3000). V, L and D are never repeated.
  - SUBTRACT when a smaller symbol comes before a larger one. Only six pairs are
    allowed: IV = 4, IX = 9, XL = 40, XC = 90, CD = 400, CM = 900.
  - Number to Roman: split the number into thousands, hundreds, tens and ones
    and write each part: 1994 = 1000 + 900 + 90 + 4 = M CM XC IV = MCMXCIV.
  - Roman to number: read from the left, adding, but take away a symbol that
    stands before a bigger one: XLIX = 40 + 9 = 49.
  - The biggest number with these rules is 3999 = MMMCMXCIX.
  - Roman numerals are used on clock faces, for chapters, classes, kings and
    queens, and for years on buildings and in films: MMXXVI = 2026.

PART A — DESCRIPTIVE
--------------------
D1. Dadaji's wall clock has Roman numerals. Read the numbers on the clock face
    from 1 to 12.
                  XII
             XI         I
           X               II
          IX       •       III
           VIII            IV
             VII        V
                  VI
    Answer: I, II, III, IV, V, VI, VII, VIII, IX, X, XI, XII.
    Teacher explains: Start at the top: XII is 12 (10 + 1 + 1). Going clockwise:
    I, II, III are 1, 2, 3 made of ones. IV is 5 − 1 = 4, V is 5, and VI, VII,
    VIII are 5 + 1, 5 + 2, 5 + 3. IX is 10 − 1 = 9, X is 10, and XI is 10 + 1.
    Some old clocks show IIII instead of IV for 4; this is a clock-maker's
    custom, but the correct numeral is IV. Now you try: the short hand points at
    VII and the long hand at XII. What time is it? (7 o'clock)

D2. Why is VIII equal to 8, XVI equal to 16 and LXII equal to 62?
    Answer: When symbols go from biggest to smallest we add them:
    VIII = 5 + 3 = 8, XVI = 10 + 5 + 1 = 16, LXII = 50 + 10 + 2 = 62.
    Teacher explains: Read from left to right. If each symbol is the same as or
    bigger than the one after it, just add all the values. Say the values aloud
    as you point: "fifty, sixty, sixty-one, sixty-two". This is the ADDING rule.
    Now you try: LXXVII and CLVI. (77; 156)

D3. Why do we write 4 as IV and not IIII?
    Answer: Because I placed before V means "one less than five":
    IV = 5 − 1 = 4. A symbol may not be repeated four times.
    Teacher explains: When a SMALLER symbol stands just before a BIGGER one, we
    subtract it. So IV = 4 and VI = 6: the I on the left takes one away, the I
    on the right adds one. In the same way IX = 10 − 1 = 9, and XI = 11. This
    SUBTRACTING rule keeps numerals short. Now you try: write 9 and 14. (IX;
    XIV)

D4. Rohan wrote 49 as IL (one less than fifty). Why is this wrong? What is the
    correct numeral?
    Answer: Only six subtraction pairs are allowed, and IL is not one of them.
    49 = 40 + 9 = XL + IX = XLIX.
    Teacher explains: The allowed pairs are IV (4), IX (9), XL (40), XC (90), CD
    (400) and CM (900). I can only go before V and X; X only before L and C; C
    only before D and M. To write 49, split it by place value: 40 and 9. 40 = XL
    and 9 = IX, so 49 = XLIX. Check: 50 − 10 + 10 − 1 = 49. Now you try: 99.
    (XCIX, not IC)

D5. A new school building has the year it was built on the wall. It was built in
    2026. How is 2026 written in Roman numerals?
        Thousands | Hundreds | Tens | Ones
           2000   |    0     |  20  |  6
            MM    |  (none)  |  XX  |  VI
    Answer: MMXXVI.
    Teacher explains: Split by place value: 2026 = 2000 + 20 + 6. 2000 = MM,
    there are no hundreds (Roman numerals have no zero, so we write nothing),
    20 = XX and 6 = VI. Put them together: MMXXVI. Check:
    1000 + 1000 + 10 + 10 + 5 + 1 = 2026. Now you try: 2008. (MMVIII)

D6. What year is MCMXCIV?
    Answer: 1994. M + CM + XC + IV = 1000 + 900 + 90 + 4 = 1994.
    Teacher explains: Break the numeral into chunks from the left. M = 1000.
    Next C is before M, a bigger symbol, so CM is a pair: 900. Then X before C:
    XC = 90. Then I before V: IV = 4. Add the chunks:
    1000 + 900 + 90 + 4 = 1994. Underline each subtraction pair first; then
    everything else is just adding. Now you try: MCMLXXXV. (1985)

D7. Sonu says VV = 10 and XXXX = 40. What rules has he broken?
    Answer: V, L and D are never repeated (10 is X). No symbol is repeated more
    than three times (40 is XL).
    Teacher explains: Two fives make ten, and there is already a symbol for ten:
    X. In the same way LL would be C and DD would be M. For 40, four X's are too
    many; use the subtraction pair XL (50 − 10). So the numbers 1 to 10 go I,
    II, III, IV, V, VI, VII, VIII, IX, X, and the tens go X, XX, XXX, XL, L, LX,
    LXX, LXXX, XC, C. Now you try: correct CCCC. (CD = 400)

D8. A storybook has Chapters XIV, XIX and XXIV. What are these numbers? Which
    chapter comes after Chapter XXXIX?
    Answer: XIV = 14, XIX = 19, XXIV = 24. After XXXIX (39) comes XL (40).
    Teacher explains: XIV = X + IV = 10 + 4. XIX = X + IX = 10 + 9.
    XXIV = XX + IV = 20 + 4. For the next chapter after 39: 40 is not XXXX but
    XL. Watch out when counting in Roman numerals at 4, 9, 40, 90, 400 and 900:
    those are the places where the subtraction pairs come in. Now you try: which
    chapter comes after LXXXIX? (XC = 90)

D9. Which of these are NOT correct Roman numerals: XC, IC, XLV, IIV, VX, MMM?
    Answer: IC, IIV and VX are not correct. (XC = 90, XLV = 45, MMM = 3000 are
    correct.)
    Teacher explains: IC: I may only go before V or X, so 99 must be XCIX. IIV:
    only one small symbol can be taken away, so 3 is III. VX: V is never used
    for subtracting, so 5 is just V. Check any numeral by changing it to a
    number and back: if you do not get the same numeral, it is not correct. Now
    you try: is XIIII correct? (no, 14 = XIV)

D10. What is the biggest number you can write with Roman numerals using these
     rules? Can you write 0?
     Answer: 3999 = MMMCMXCIX. There is no Roman numeral for 0.
     Teacher explains: M can be repeated only three times: MMM = 3000. Then the
     biggest hundreds is CM (900), the biggest tens XC (90) and the biggest ones
     IX (9): MMMCMXCIX = 3000 + 900 + 90 + 9 = 3999. The Romans had no zero;
     that is why places with no value are simply left out, as in MMXXVI. Our
     Hindu-Arabic numerals, with the zero that came from India, make place value
     and calculation much easier. Now you try: 3888. (MMMDCCCLXXXVIII)

D11. India became independent in 1947. Write 1947 in Roman numerals. How many
     years is it from 1947 to 2026? Write that in Roman numerals too.
     Answer: 1947 = MCMXLVII. 2026 − 1947 = 79 = LXXIX.
     Teacher explains:
     1947 = 1000 + 900 + 40 + 7 = M + CM + XL + VII = MCMXLVII. For the years in
     between, subtract in ordinary numbers: 2026 − 1947 = 79. Then
     79 = 70 + 9 = LXX + IX = LXXIX. Also: 1950 (the Republic) = MCML. Now you
     try: 1857. (MDCCCLVII)

D12. Work out XXIV + XVIII and write the answer in Roman numerals.
     Answer: 24 + 18 = 42 = XLII.
     Teacher explains: Adding Roman numerals directly is very hard, so change
     them into our numbers first: XXIV = 24 and XVIII = 18. Add: 24 + 18 = 42.
     Change back: 42 = 40 + 2 = XL + II = XLII. This is why the place-value
     system we use today replaced Roman numerals for calculating. Now you try:
     L − XIII. (50 − 13 = 37 = XXXVII)

PART B — MATHEMATICAL ONLY
--------------------------
M1. Write in Roman numerals: every number from 1 to 25.
    a) 1    b) 2    c) 3    d) 4    e) 5    f) 6    g) 7
    h) 8    i) 9    j) 10   k) 11   l) 12   m) 13   n) 14
    o) 15   p) 16   q) 17   r) 18   s) 19   t) 20   u) 21
    v) 22   w) 23   x) 24   y) 25
    Answers: a) I   b) II   c) III   d) IV   e) V   f) VI   g) VII   h) VIII
             i) IX   j) X   k) XI   l) XII   m) XIII   n) XIV   o) XV   p) XVI
             q) XVII   r) XVIII   s) XIX   t) XX   u) XXI   v) XXII   w) XXIII
             x) XXIV   y) XXV

M2. Write in Roman numerals: every number from 26 to 50.
    a) 26   b) 27   c) 28   d) 29   e) 30   f) 31   g) 32
    h) 33   i) 34   j) 35   k) 36   l) 37   m) 38   n) 39
    o) 40   p) 41   q) 42   r) 43   s) 44   t) 45   u) 46
    v) 47   w) 48   x) 49   y) 50
    Answers: a) XXVI   b) XXVII   c) XXVIII   d) XXIX   e) XXX   f) XXXI
             g) XXXII   h) XXXIII   i) XXXIV   j) XXXV   k) XXXVI   l) XXXVII
             m) XXXVIII   n) XXXIX   o) XL   p) XLI   q) XLII   r) XLIII
             s) XLIV   t) XLV   u) XLVI   v) XLVII   w) XLVIII   x) XLIX   y) L

M3. Write as an ordinary number.
    a) VII     b) XLIV    c) XXXV    d) IV      e) XXIII   f) V
    g) IX      h) XII     i) XXV     j) XLVI    k) XXVII   l) XVII
    m) XIV     n) XXXVI   o) II      p) VIII    q) XV      r) XLV
    s) X       t) XLVII   u) XLIX    v) XX      w) XVI     x) XXIX
    Answers: a) 7   b) 44   c) 35   d) 4   e) 23   f) 5   g) 9   h) 12   i) 25
             j) 46   k) 27   l) 17   m) 14   n) 36   o) 2   p) 8   q) 15   r) 45
             s) 10   t) 47   u) 49   v) 20   w) 16   x) 29

M4. Write in Roman numerals: tens, hundreds and thousands.
    a) 10     b) 20     c) 30     d) 40     e) 50     f) 60
    g) 70     h) 80     i) 90     j) 100    k) 200    l) 300
    m) 400    n) 500    o) 600    p) 700    q) 800    r) 900
    s) 1000   t) 2000   u) 3000
    Answers: a) X   b) XX   c) XXX   d) XL   e) L   f) LX   g) LXX   h) LXXX
             i) XC   j) C   k) CC   l) CCC   m) CD   n) D   o) DC   p) DCC
             q) DCCC   r) CM   s) M   t) MM   u) MMM

M5. Write in Roman numerals (51 to 100).
    a) 51   b) 54   c) 58   d) 59   e) 60   f) 64
    g) 66   h) 69   i) 70   j) 73   k) 77   l) 79
    m) 84   n) 88   o) 89   p) 90   q) 94   r) 95
    s) 98   t) 99
    Answers: a) LI   b) LIV   c) LVIII   d) LIX   e) LX   f) LXIV   g) LXVI
             h) LXIX   i) LXX   j) LXXIII   k) LXXVII   l) LXXIX   m) LXXXIV
             n) LXXXVIII   o) LXXXIX   p) XC   q) XCIV   r) XCV   s) XCVIII
             t) XCIX

M6. Write as an ordinary number.
    a) CCXXXIV     b) CDLXIX      c) LI          d) CDLVIII     e) CLXXXV
    f) CCVII       g) CCXLII      h) CCVI        i) CDLXXI      j) CDLXXXIV
    k) CCCLXXXII   l) CXXV        m) CCLXXXV     n) CCCXIII     o) CXIII
    p) CCCLVIII    q) CDLXXVII    r) CCXVII      s) CLIV        t) LXXXIII
    Answers: a) 234   b) 469   c) 51   d) 458   e) 185   f) 207   g) 242
             h) 206   i) 471   j) 484   k) 382   l) 125   m) 285   n) 313
             o) 113   p) 358   q) 477   r) 217   s) 154   t) 83

M7. Write in Roman numerals (100 to 1000).
    a) 104    b) 149    c) 199    d) 234    e) 290
    f) 345    g) 400    h) 444    i) 499    j) 500
    k) 555    l) 606    m) 649    n) 777    o) 890
    p) 900    q) 944    r) 990    s) 999    t) 1000
    Answers: a) CIV   b) CXLIX   c) CXCIX   d) CCXXXIV   e) CCXC   f) CCCXLV
             g) CD   h) CDXLIV   i) CDXCIX   j) D   k) DLV   l) DCVI   m) DCXLIX
             n) DCCLXXVII   o) DCCCXC   p) CM   q) CMXLIV   r) CMXC   s) CMXCIX
             t) M

M8. Write in Roman numerals (up to 3999, including years).
    a) 1066   b) 1492   c) 1857   d) 1947
    e) 1950   f) 1999   g) 2000   h) 2008
    i) 2014   j) 2026   k) 1111   l) 1234
    m) 1776   n) 2500   o) 2999   p) 3000
    q) 3333   r) 3888   s) 3999   t) 1600
    Answers: a) MLXVI   b) MCDXCII   c) MDCCCLVII   d) MCMXLVII   e) MCML
             f) MCMXCIX   g) MM   h) MMVIII   i) MMXIV   j) MMXXVI   k) MCXI
             l) MCCXXXIV   m) MDCCLXXVI   n) MMD   o) MMCMXCIX   p) MMM
             q) MMMCCCXXXIII   r) MMMDCCCLXXXVIII   s) MMMCMXCIX   t) MDC

M9. Write as an ordinary number.
    a) MCDLX        b) MCCCLXXI     c) MMMCMXCIX
    d) MCCLXXI      e) MMMDCXIII    f) MMMDCCCXIX
    g) MMMCMXLIX    h) MDCIII       i) MMCCXXII
    j) MMMDCXXIII   k) MMMCDXCIX    l) MMMCXIX
    m) MCDXLIV      n) MCMXC        o) MMMDCLXI
