TEACHING TIPS
  Struggling: Make a real "machine" from a box with a slot at each end; one
  person posts a number card in, the other applies the secret rule and posts the
  answer out. Use one-step + and × rules first, and always say the rule out
  loud. For patterns, write the jumps between terms in small numbers above the
  sequence. Harder: three-step machines; machines that are the same (× 2
  then × 3 is the same as × 6); find a rule from pairs that are not in order;
  describe the nth term of a growing shape pattern in words and use it for the
  100th term; invent a secret rule for a friend to guess.


================================================================================
TOPIC 4.40 — SIMPLE UNKNOWNS IN EQUATIONS (boxes/symbols for numbers)
================================================================================
WHAT THE CHILD MUST LEARN
  - A box □ or a symbol (▲, ●, ■) stands for a number we do not know yet. The
    equals sign means both sides have the SAME VALUE, like a balance that is
    level.
  - Find the missing number with the OPPOSITE operation (fact families):
    □ + 27 = 63 so □ = 63 − 27 = 36; □ − 18 = 27 so □ = 27 + 18 = 45; 8 × □ = 56
    so □ = 56 ÷ 8 = 7; □ ÷ 4 = 9 so □ = 9 × 4 = 36.
  - Take care when the box is the number taken away or the number we divide by:
    45 − □ = 18 so □ = 45 − 18 = 27; 72 ÷ □ = 8 so □ = 72 ÷ 8 = 9.
  - ALWAYS check by putting the number back into the box: 36 + 27 = 63.
  - Two-step: 3 × □ + 4 = 19. Undo the last step first: 19 − 4 = 15, then
    15 ÷ 3 = 5. On a balance, take the same away from both sides and it stays
    level.
  - In one puzzle, the SAME symbol always stands for the SAME number: ▲ + ▲ = 14
    means ▲ = 7. Different symbols may stand for different numbers. Solve the
    easiest equation first, then use that number in the others.
  - Both sides can have sums: 25 + □ = 30 + 12. Work out the known side first
    (42), then solve 25 + □ = 42.
  - Write a word problem as an equation with a box, then solve it.

PART A — DESCRIPTIVE
--------------------
D1. Riya had some marbles. Her friend gave her 27 more, and now she has 63. How
    many did she have at first? Write it with a box.
       |<------------------ 63 ------------------>|
       |        □             |        27          |
    Answer: □ + 27 = 63, so □ = 36. She had 36 marbles.
    Teacher explains: The bar shows the whole (63) made of two parts: the
    unknown part and 27. To find a part, take the known part away from the
    whole: 63 − 27 = 36. Check: 36 + 27 = 63 (yes).
    Common mistake: adding 63 + 27 because the story says "gave her more". Ask:
    "Is the missing number the whole or a part?" Now you try: □ + 45 = 100. (55)

D2. A bus had 45 passengers. Some got off at the market, and 18 were left. How
    many got off? Solve 45 − □ = 18.
    Answer: □ = 27. 27 passengers got off.
    Teacher explains: Here the box is the number TAKEN AWAY. The whole is 45;
    the two parts are the people who got off (□) and the people left (18). So
    □ = 45 − 18 = 27. Check: 45 − 27 = 18 (yes).
    Common mistake: 45 + 18 = 63 — but you cannot take 63 away from 45! Think of
    the fact family: 18 + 27 = 45, 45 − 27 = 18, 45 − 18 = 27. Now you try:
    80 − □ = 35. (45)

D3. Ravi spent ₹18 on a notebook and has ₹27 left. How much did he have at
    first? Solve □ − 18 = 27.
    Answer: □ = 45. He had ₹45.
    Teacher explains: Now the box is the WHOLE, the number we started with.
    Money spent and money left together make what he had: □ = 27 + 18 = 45.
    Adding undoes taking away. Check: 45 − 18 = 27 (yes).
    Compare with D2: in 45 − □ = 18 we subtract, but in □ − 18 = 27 we add.
    Always ask what the box stands for. Now you try: □ − 150 = 350. (500)

D4. Amma packs 56 laddoos into 8 boxes with the same number in each. How many
    laddoos are in each box? Solve 8 × □ = 56.
    Answer: □ = 7. There are 7 laddoos in each box.
    Teacher explains: 8 times what makes 56? Use the opposite of ×, which is ÷:
    □ = 56 ÷ 8 = 7. Or say the 8 times table: 8, 16, 24, 32, 40, 48, 56 — that
    is 7 eights. Check: 8 × 7 = 56 (yes).
    The box can be on either side of ×: □ × 8 = 56 has the same answer, because
    7 × 8 = 8 × 7. Now you try: 6 × □ = 54. (9)

D5. Some mangoes are shared equally among 4 children, and each child gets 9. How
    many mangoes were there? Then solve 72 ÷ □ = 8.
    Answer: □ ÷ 4 = 9, so □ = 36 mangoes. In 72 ÷ □ = 8, □ = 9.
    Teacher explains: In □ ÷ 4 = 9, the box is the whole that was shared. Four
    children with 9 each: □ = 9 × 4 = 36. Multiplying undoes dividing. Check:
    36 ÷ 4 = 9 (yes).
    In 72 ÷ □ = 8, the box is the number we divide BY. 72 shared into how many
    groups gives 8 in each? □ = 72 ÷ 8 = 9, because 8 × 9 = 72. Check:
    72 ÷ 9 = 8 (yes). Now you try: □ ÷ 6 = 7. (42)

D6. The two pans of this balance are level. What number goes in the box?
            25 + □              30 + 12
          -----------         -----------
               |                   |
               +---------+---------+
                         |
                        / \
    Answer: □ = 17, because 25 + 17 = 42 and 30 + 12 = 42.
    Teacher explains: Level means both sides have the SAME value. Work out the
    side we know: 30 + 12 = 42. Now 25 + □ = 42, so □ = 42 − 25 = 17. Check:
    25 + 17 = 42 = 30 + 12 (yes).
    Common mistake: writing 25 + □ = 30 and answering 5, forgetting the 12. The
    equals sign is not "the answer comes next" — it means "is the same as". Now
    you try: 40 + 15 = □ + 20. (35)

D7. Mohan has 3 bags with the same number of sweets in each and 4 loose sweets.
    He has 19 sweets altogether. How many are in each bag? Solve 3 × □ + 4 = 19.
    Answer: □ = 5. There are 5 sweets in each bag.
    Teacher explains: Undo the steps in reverse order. The last thing done
    was + 4, so take away the 4 loose sweets: 19 − 4 = 15. Now 3 × □ = 15, so
    □ = 15 ÷ 3 = 5. On a balance: take 4 away from BOTH pans and it stays level:
    3 × □ = 15. Check: 3 × 5 + 4 = 19 (yes).
    Common mistake: dividing first (19 ÷ 3) — it does not even work out. Now you
    try: 5 × □ − 3 = 32. (7)

D8. Solve the symbol puzzle: ▲ + ▲ = 14 and ▲ + ● = 10.
    Answer: ▲ = 7 and ● = 3.
    Teacher explains: Start with the equation that has only ONE kind of symbol:
    ▲ + ▲ = 14. Two equal numbers make 14, so each is half of 14: ▲ = 7. The
    same ▲ is 7 everywhere in this puzzle, so put 7 into the second equation:
    7 + ● = 10, so ● = 3. Check both: 7 + 7 = 14 (yes) and 7 + 3 = 10 (yes).
    Now you try: ■ + ■ + ■ = 18 and ■ − ● = 2. (■ = 6, ● = 4)

D9. On a balance, 2 equal melons and a 100 g weight balance 1 melon and a 350 g
    weight. How heavy is one melon?
         ● ● + 100 g   =   ● + 350 g
    Answer: One melon weighs 250 g.
    Teacher explains: Take the same thing off both pans and the balance stays
    level. Take one melon off each side: ● + 100 g = 350 g. Now take 100 g off
    each side: ● = 250 g. Check: 250 + 250 + 100 = 600 and 250 + 350 = 600
    (yes).
    This is the balance idea: what we do to one side we must do to the other.
    Now you try: 3 × ▲ + 2 = ▲ + 12. (▲ = 5)

D10. A packet of 6 pens costs ₹48. What does one pen cost? Write an equation
     with a box, and solve it.
     Answer: 6 × □ = 48, so □ = 8. One pen costs ₹8.
     Teacher explains: Say the story in maths words: "6 pens, each costing □
     rupees, cost 48 rupees" becomes 6 × □ = 48. Then solve with the opposite
     operation: □ = 48 ÷ 6 = 8. Check: 6 × 8 = 48 (yes). Writing the box
     equation first helps the child choose the right operation.
     Now you try: 5 friends share ₹75 equally. Write and solve the equation.
     (75 ÷ 5 = □, □ = 15)

D11. Solve □ + 1250 = 4000 and 7 × □ = 490.
     Answer: □ = 2750 and □ = 70.
     Teacher explains: Big numbers work the same way. □ = 4000 − 1250 = 2750;
     check: 2750 + 1250 = 4000 (yes). For 7 × □ = 490, think of 7 × 7 = 49, so
     7 × 70 = 490: □ = 490 ÷ 7 = 70. Check: 7 × 70 = 490 (yes).
           3 9 10 10
           4 0  0  0
         − 1 2  5  0
         -----------
           2 7  5  0
     Now you try: □ − 2500 = 1500. (4000)

D12. Sonu says: "In □ + □ = 10, one box can be 6 and the other 4." Is he right?
     And how many answers does ▲ + ● = 8 have?
     Answer: No — both boxes are the same, so □ = 5. ▲ + ● = 8 has many answers,
     such as 1 and 7, 2 and 6, 3 and 5, 4 and 4.
     Teacher explains: The same symbol in one equation stands for the same
     number, so □ + □ = 10 means 5 + 5. But ▲ and ● are different symbols, so
     they CAN be different (they can also be the same, 4 + 4). With one equation
     and two different symbols there are many answers; we need a second clue to
     find just one.
     Now you try: find three answers for ▲ + ● = 8 with ▲ bigger than ●. (for
     example 5 and 3, 6 and 2, 7 and 1)

PART B — MATHEMATICAL ONLY
--------------------------
M1. Find the number in the box. Check by putting it back.
    a) □ + 27 = 63      b) □ + 45 = 100     c) □ + 8 = 15
    d) □ + 19 = 50      e) □ + 36 = 91      f) □ + 0 = 24
    g) □ + 125 = 400    h) □ + 64 = 64      i) □ + 99 = 180
    j) □ + 250 = 1000   k) 14 + □ = 30      l) 38 + □ = 75
    m) 7 + □ = 16       n) 56 + □ = 102     o) 300 + □ = 725
    p) 48 + □ = 48      q) 1 + □ = 1000     r) 65 + □ = 140
    s) 17 + □ = 60      t) 205 + □ = 350
    Answers: a) 36   b) 55   c) 7   d) 31   e) 55   f) 24   g) 275   h) 0
             i) 81   j) 750   k) 16   l) 37   m) 9   n) 46   o) 425   p) 0
             q) 999   r) 75   s) 43   t) 145

M2. Find the number in the box. Is the box the whole, or the number taken away?
    a) □ − 18 = 27      b) □ − 9 = 16       c) □ − 35 = 65
    d) □ − 150 = 350    e) □ − 7 = 0        f) □ − 48 = 52
    g) □ − 99 = 1       h) □ − 240 = 760    i) □ − 65 = 35
    j) □ − 1000 = 500   k) 45 − □ = 18      l) 80 − □ = 35
    m) 23 − □ = 9       n) 100 − □ = 64     o) 72 − □ = 72
    p) 60 − □ = 0       q) 500 − □ = 125    r) 91 − □ = 37
    s) 1000 − □ = 1     t) 84 − □ = 48
    Answers: a) 45   b) 25   c) 100   d) 500   e) 7   f) 100   g) 100   h) 1000
             i) 100   j) 1500   k) 27   l) 45   m) 14   n) 36   o) 0   p) 60
             q) 375   r) 54   s) 999   t) 36

M3. Find the number in the box (every table from 2 to 12).
    a) 2 × □ = 14     b) □ × 3 = 24     c) 4 × □ = 24     d) □ × 5 = 45
    e) 6 × □ = 54     f) □ × 7 = 56     g) 8 × □ = 56     h) □ × 9 = 54
    i) 10 × □ = 40    j) □ × 11 = 55    k) 12 × □ = 84    l) □ × 2 = 24
    m) 3 × □ = 33     n) □ × 4 = 36     o) 5 × □ = 0      p) □ × 6 = 72
    q) 7 × □ = 49     r) □ × 8 = 64     s) 9 × □ = 81     t) □ × 10 = 100
    u) 11 × □ = 121   v) □ × 12 = 144   w) 1 × □ = 13     x) □ × 7 = 42
    Answers: a) 7   b) 8   c) 6   d) 9   e) 9   f) 8   g) 7   h) 6   i) 4   j) 5
             k) 7   l) 12   m) 11   n) 9   o) 0   p) 12   q) 7   r) 8   s) 9
             t) 10   u) 11   v) 12   w) 13   x) 6

M4. Find the number in the box. Is the box the whole, or the number we divide
    by?
    a) □ ÷ 4 = 9     b) □ ÷ 6 = 7     c) □ ÷ 3 = 12    d) □ ÷ 8 = 5
    e) □ ÷ 9 = 9     f) □ ÷ 5 = 20    g) □ ÷ 7 = 11    h) □ ÷ 10 = 10
    i) □ ÷ 12 = 4    j) □ ÷ 2 = 50    k) 72 ÷ □ = 8    l) 54 ÷ □ = 6
    m) 63 ÷ □ = 9    n) 40 ÷ □ = 5    o) 96 ÷ □ = 12   p) 35 ÷ □ = 7
    q) 81 ÷ □ = 9    r) 100 ÷ □ = 4   s) 48 ÷ □ = 48   t) 88 ÷ □ = 11
    Answers: a) 36   b) 42   c) 36   d) 40   e) 81   f) 100   g) 77   h) 100
             i) 48   j) 100   k) 9   l) 9   m) 7   n) 8   o) 8   p) 5   q) 9
             r) 25   s) 1   t) 8

M5. Bigger numbers, the same ideas.
    a) □ + 1250 = 4000       b) 7 × □ = 490           c) □ − 2500 = 1500
    d) □ ÷ 10 = 36           e) 9000 − □ = 4500       f) □ × 100 = 3500
    g) 4800 ÷ □ = 12         h) 3999 + □ = 10,000     i) □ ÷ 25 = 4
    j) 60 × □ = 3600         k) □ − 999 = 1           l) 12 × □ = 144
    m) 2 × □ = 1000          n) □ + 675 = 1000        o) 250 × □ = 1000
    p) □ ÷ 8 = 125           q) 5050 − □ = 50         r) □ × 11 = 132
    s) 45,000 + □ = 50,000   t) □ − 1 = 99,999
    Answers: a) 2750   b) 70   c) 4000   d) 360   e) 4500   f) 35   g) 400
             h) 6001   i) 100   j) 60   k) 1000   l) 12   m) 500   n) 325   o) 4
             p) 1000   q) 5000   r) 12   s) 5000   t) 100,000

M6. Both sides must have the same value. Work out the known side first.
    a) 25 + □ = 30 + 12      b) 40 + 15 = □ + 20      c) □ − 8 = 10 + 5
    d) 6 × □ = 4 × 9         e) □ + 19 = 50 − 11      f) 3 × 8 = □ × 4
    g) 100 − □ = 45 + 5      h) □ ÷ 3 = 2 × 5         i) 7 + 8 + □ = 30
    j) 12 × 5 = 30 × □       k) □ + □ = 9 + 9         l) 72 ÷ 8 = □ − 11
    m) 35 + 65 = □ × 10      n) □ × 3 = 50 − 14       o) 48 ÷ □ = 12 ÷ 2
    p) 200 + □ = 150 + 150
    Answers: a) 17   b) 35   c) 23   d) 6   e) 20   f) 6   g) 50   h) 30   i) 15
             j) 2   k) 9   l) 20   m) 10   n) 12   o) 8   p) 100

M7. Two steps: undo the last step first.
    a) 3 × □ + 4 = 19      b) 5 × □ − 3 = 32      c) 2 × □ + 1 = 21
    d) 4 × □ + 3 = 31      e) 6 × □ − 6 = 30      f) 10 × □ + 5 = 85
    g) 7 × □ − 2 = 47      h) 9 × □ + 9 = 90      i) □ ÷ 2 + 6 = 10
    j) □ ÷ 5 − 1 = 3       k) □ × 8 + 7 = 71      l) □ × 3 − 10 = 20
    m) 2 × □ + 50 = 150    n) 12 × □ − 4 = 140    o) □ ÷ 4 + 4 = 8
    p) 11 × □ + 11 = 121
    Answers: a) 5   b) 7   c) 10   d) 7   e) 6   f) 8   g) 7   h) 9   i) 8
             j) 20   k) 8   l) 10   m) 50   n) 12   o) 16   p) 10

M8. Symbol puzzles: the same symbol is the same number.
    a) ▲ + ▲ = 14;  ▲ + ● = 10           b) ● + ● = 20;  ● − ▲ = 4
    c) ■ × 2 = 16;  ■ + ▲ = 15           d) ▲ + ▲ + ▲ = 27;  ▲ − ● = 5
    e) ● × ● = 36;  ● + ■ = 11           f) ▲ + ● = 12;  ▲ − ● = 2
    g) ■ + ■ = 30;  ■ ÷ ● = 3            h) ▲ × 4 = 32;  ● × ▲ = 56
    i) ● + ● + ● + ● = 40;  ● + ▲ = 25   j) ■ − ▲ = 0;  ■ + ▲ = 18
    k) ▲ × ▲ = 81;  ▲ + ■ = 20           l) ● + ▲ = 15;  ● = ▲ + ▲
    Answers: a) ▲ = 7, ● = 3   b) ▲ = 6, ● = 10   c) ▲ = 7, ■ = 8
             d) ▲ = 9, ● = 4   e) ● = 6, ■ = 5   f) ▲ = 7, ● = 5
             g) ● = 5, ■ = 15   h) ▲ = 8, ● = 7   i) ▲ = 15, ● = 10
             j) ▲ = 9, ■ = 9   k) ▲ = 9, ■ = 11   l) ▲ = 5, ● = 10

M9. Three symbols. Start with the equation that has only one kind of symbol.
    a) ▲ + ▲ = 10;  ▲ + ● = 12;  ● + ■ = 20
    b) ■ × 3 = 24;  ■ + ▲ = 17;  ▲ − ● = 5
    c) ● + ● + ● = 21;  ● × ▲ = 42;  ▲ + ■ = 16
    d) ▲ + ● + ■ = 20;  ▲ + ▲ = 8;  ● = 2 × ▲
    e) ■ − ● = 3;  ● + ● = 18;  ■ + ▲ = 20
    f) ▲ × ▲ = 25;  ▲ × ● = 45;  ● − ■ = 7
    g) ● ÷ 4 = 5;  ● − ▲ = 11;  ▲ × ■ = 36
    h) ■ + ■ + ■ + ■ = 48;  ■ ÷ ▲ = 4;  ▲ + ● = 10
    Answers: a) ▲ = 5, ● = 7, ■ = 13   b) ▲ = 9, ● = 4, ■ = 8
             c) ▲ = 6, ● = 7, ■ = 10   d) ▲ = 4, ● = 8, ■ = 8
             e) ▲ = 8, ● = 9, ■ = 12   f) ▲ = 5, ● = 9, ■ = 2
             g) ▲ = 9, ● = 20, ■ = 4   h) ▲ = 3, ● = 7, ■ = 12

M10. Balance puzzles: take the same away from both sides.
     a) 3 × ▲ + 2 = ▲ + 12      b) ● + ● + 5 = ● + 13
     c) 4 × ■ = ■ + 18          d) ▲ + 20 = 3 × ▲
     e) 5 × ● − 4 = 2 × ● + 8   f) ■ + ■ + ■ = ■ + 30
     g) 2 × ▲ + 7 = ▲ + 16      h) 6 × ● = 4 × ● + 14
     i) ■ + 45 = 4 × ■          j) 10 × ▲ = 8 × ▲ + 50
     k) 3 × ● + 1 = ● + 21      l) 7 × ■ − 5 = 5 × ■ + 5
     Answers: a) ▲ = 5   b) ● = 8   c) ■ = 6   d) ▲ = 10   e) ● = 4   f) ■ = 15
              g) ▲ = 9   h) ● = 7   i) ■ = 15   j) ▲ = 25   k) ● = 10   l) ■ = 5

M11. True or false? If false, give the right number.
     a) □ + 27 = 63, □ = 46        b) 45 − □ = 18, □ = 27
     c) 8 × □ = 56, □ = 7          d) □ ÷ 4 = 9, □ = 36
     e) □ − 18 = 27, □ = 9         f) 72 ÷ □ = 8, □ = 9
     g) 3 × □ + 4 = 19, □ = 5      h) □ + 150 = 400, □ = 350
     i) 9 × □ = 63, □ = 8          j) 100 − □ = 37, □ = 63
     k) □ ÷ 6 = 8, □ = 48          l) 2 × □ − 1 = 15, □ = 7
     m) 25 + □ = 30 + 12, □ = 17   n) □ × □ = 64, □ = 8
     o) □ + □ = 50, □ = 20         p) 60 ÷ □ = 12, □ = 6
     Answers: a) false (36)   b) true   c) true   d) true   e) false (45)
              f) true   g) true   h) false (250)   i) false (7)   j) true
              k) true   l) false (8)   m) true   n) true   o) false (25)
              p) false (5)

M12. The same box means the same number.
     a) □ + □ = 50            b) □ × □ = 64            c) □ + □ + □ = 36
     d) □ × □ = 100           e) □ + □ = 3 × 8         f) □ × □ = 1
     g) 2 × □ = □ + 9         h) □ + □ + □ + □ = 100   i) □ × □ = 144
     j) □ + □ = □ + 15        k) □ × □ = 0             l) □ + □ = 1000
     m) □ × □ = 49            n) □ + □ + □ = 3 × 3     o) □ × 3 = □ + 30
     p) □ × □ = 121

              j) 15   k) 0   l) 500   m) 7   n) 3   o) 15   p) 11

TEACHING TIPS
  Struggling: Use a real balance or a see-saw drawing and counters: hide some
  counters under a cup (the box) and keep both sides level. Say each equation as
  a question in words ("What plus 27 makes 63?") and draw a part-whole bar to
  decide whether to add or subtract. Always check by putting the number back.
  Harder: three-step equations (2 × □ + 3 × □ = 45); symbol puzzles with four
  symbols; unknowns on both sides with bigger numbers; write your own word
  problem for 7 × □ − 5 = 51; puzzles with no answer or many answers, and
  explain why.


================================================================================
TOPIC 4.41 — ESTIMATION AND CHECKING REASONABLENESS OF ANSWERS
================================================================================
WHAT THE CHILD MUST LEARN
  - An ESTIMATE is a quick "about" answer. We write ≈ for "is about":
    487 + 312 ≈ 500 + 300 = 800. Estimate BEFORE working out, then check the
    exact answer is close.
  - Round to the nearest 10, 100 or 1000 and then calculate. Rounding to a
    bigger place is quicker; rounding to a smaller place is closer.
  - Rounding to the LEADING digit (one non-zero digit): 487 ≈ 500, 6215 ≈ 6000,
    38 ≈ 40. FRONT-END estimation keeps only the first digit and drops the rest
    (3456 + 2781 → 3000 + 2000 = 5000), then ADJUSTS with the leftover parts
    (456 + 781 is about 1200, so about 6200).
  - Multiplication: round the big number (or both numbers):
    38 × 6 ≈ 40 × 6 = 240; 47 × 23 ≈ 50 × 20 = 1000.
  - Division: change the number being divided to a nearby COMPATIBLE number that
    divides exactly: 358 ÷ 6 ≈ 360 ÷ 6 = 60; 2395 ÷ 8 ≈ 2400 ÷ 8 = 300.
  - A RANGE tells us where the exact answer must be: 38 × 6 is between
    30 × 6 = 180 and 40 × 6 = 240.
  - Is the answer REASONABLE? Compare it with an estimate. If 398 + 207 is
    written as 905, the estimate 600 shows a mistake. Estimates catch
    place-value slips and wrong operations.
  - Know when an estimate is enough ("about how many people came?") and when an
    exact answer is needed (change from a shop, medicine). When checking if
    money is enough, round UP so the estimate is safe.

PART A — DESCRIPTIVE
--------------------
D1. A shop sold 487 kg of rice on Monday and 312 kg on Tuesday. About how much
    did it sell in the two days? Then find the exact amount.
    Answer: About 800 kg (500 + 300). Exact: 799 kg.
    Teacher explains: Round each number to the nearest hundred: 487 is between
    400 and 500 and nearer to 500 (the tens digit 8 is 5 or more, so round up).
    312 is nearer to 300 (the tens digit 1 is less than 5). 500 + 300 = 800, so
    487 + 312 ≈ 800.
        400 -------------------- 487 ---- 500
        300 -- 312 ------------------------ 400
    The exact answer, 799, is very close to 800, so it is reasonable. Now you
    try: 614 + 279 to the nearest hundred. (600 + 300 = 900; exact 893)

D2. A train has 912 seats. 389 seats are filled. About how many seats are empty?
    Estimate to the nearest hundred and to the nearest ten.
    Answer: Nearest hundred: 900 − 400 = 500. Nearest ten: 910 − 390 = 520.
    Exact: 523.
    Teacher explains: Estimating works for subtraction too. Nearest hundred:
    912 ≈ 900 and 389 ≈ 400, so 900 − 400 = 500. Nearest ten: 912 ≈ 910 and
    389 ≈ 390, so 910 − 390 = 520. The nearest-ten estimate is closer to the
    exact answer (523) but takes a little longer. Choose what you need.
    Now you try: 745 − 268 to the nearest ten. (750 − 270 = 480; exact 477)

D3. At a cricket match, 23,650 people came on the first day and 18,420 on the
    second day. About how many came altogether?
    Answer: About 42,000 (24,000 + 18,000). Exact: 42,070.
    Teacher explains: For big numbers, round to the nearest thousand: look at
    the hundreds digit. 23,650 has 6 hundreds, so round up to 24,000. 18,420 has
    4 hundreds, so round down to 18,000. 24 thousand + 18 thousand = 42
    thousand, which is 42,000.
    Newspapers do this: "About 42,000 fans watched the match." Now you try:
    35,280 − 12,910 to the nearest thousand. (35,000 − 13,000 = 22,000; exact
    22,370)

D4. Estimate 3456 + 2781 by front-end estimation, and then adjust.
    Answer: Front-end: 3000 + 2000 = 5000. Adjusted: about 6200. Exact: 6237.
    Teacher explains: FRONT-END means use only the front (leading) digits: 3456
    → 3000 and 2781 → 2000. 3000 + 2000 = 5000. This is always too small,
    because we dropped 456 and 781. ADJUST: 456 + 781 is about 500 + 700 = 1200,
    so the estimate becomes 5000 + 1200 = 6200.
    Front-end is fast for adding long lists, like a shopping bill. Now you try:
    front-end estimate of 4210 + 3605 + 1150. (4000 + 3000 + 1000 = 8000;
    adjusted about 9000; exact 8965)

D5. A school orders 38 boxes of chalk with 6 packets in each. About how many
    packets is that? Between which two tens-numbers times 6 must the exact
    answer be?
    Answer: 38 × 6 ≈ 40 × 6 = 240. The exact answer is between 30 × 6 = 180 and
    40 × 6 = 240. Exact: 228.
    Teacher explains: Round the 2-digit number to the nearest ten: 38 ≈ 40. Then
    40 × 6 = 240 (4 × 6 = 24, and one zero). Since 38 is between 30 and 40, the
    answer is between 30 × 6 = 180 and 40 × 6 = 240. The exact answer:
    38 × 6 = 228, which is inside the range and close to 240.
    If an answer came out as 2280 or 48, the range shows at once that it is
    wrong. Now you try: 73 × 4. (70 × 4 = 280; exact 292)

D6. A school hall has 47 rows with 23 chairs in each row. About how many chairs
    are there?
    Answer: 47 × 23 ≈ 50 × 20 = 1000. Exact: 1081.
    Teacher explains: Round BOTH numbers to the nearest ten: 47 ≈ 50 and
    23 ≈ 20. Then 50 × 20: 5 × 2 = 10 and two zeros, 1000. One number went up
    (47 to 50) and one went down (23 to 20), so the errors partly cancel and the
    estimate is quite close.
    Common mistake: counting zeros wrongly (50 × 20 = 100). Say "5 tens times 2
    tens is 10 hundreds". Now you try: 62 × 19. (60 × 20 = 1200; exact 1178)

D7. 358 sweets are shared equally among 6 children. About how many does each
    child get?
    Answer: 358 ÷ 6 ≈ 360 ÷ 6 = 60. Exact: 59 each, with 4 left over.
    Teacher explains: For division, rounding to the nearest ten or hundred may
    not help (360 is good here, but 400 ÷ 6 is not easy). Instead, find a nearby
    COMPATIBLE number — one that the divisor goes into exactly, using a known
    fact: 36 ÷ 6 = 6, so 360 ÷ 6 = 60.
    Check the exact answer: 6 × 59 = 354, and 354 + 4 = 358. Now you try:
    245 ÷ 8. (240 ÷ 8 = 30; exact 30 remainder 5)

D8. Ravi added 398 + 207 and wrote 905. Is his answer reasonable? What might
    have gone wrong?
    Answer: No. 398 + 207 ≈ 400 + 200 = 600, so 905 is far too big. The exact
    answer is 605.
    Teacher explains: An estimate is a quick check of any answer. 905 is about
    300 more than 600 — too far to be right. Ravi probably carried a 1 into the
    hundreds twice. Work it again in columns: 8 + 7 = 15, write 5 carry 1;
    9 + 0 + 1 = 10, write 0 carry 1; 3 + 2 + 1 = 6. Answer 605, close to 600.
    Make "estimate first" a habit before every written sum. Now you try: is
    612 − 289 = 423 reasonable? (no: about 600 − 300 = 300; exact 323)

D9. Meena worked out 52 × 7 on a calculator and got 3164. Is it reasonable?
    Answer: No. 52 × 7 ≈ 50 × 7 = 350, so 3164 is about ten times too big.
    Exact: 364.
    Teacher explains: A calculator is only as good as the buttons we press.
    Meena probably pressed 452 or typed a digit twice. The estimate 350 tells us
    the answer must have 3 digits, not 4. Exact: 50 × 7 = 350 and 2 × 7 = 14, so
    350 + 14 = 364.
    Always ask: "How many digits should the answer have?" Now you try: is
    8 × 49 = 392 reasonable? (yes: about 8 × 50 = 400)

D10. Mother has ₹500. She wants to buy a bedsheet for ₹189, a towel for ₹128 and
     a pillow for ₹165. Does she have enough money?
     Answer: Yes. Rounding UP: 190 + 130 + 170 = 490, which is less than 500.
     Exact total: ₹482.
     Teacher explains: When we must be SURE the money is enough, round every
     price UP. Then the estimate is more than the real total. If even the bigger
     estimate (490) fits into ₹500, the real total must fit too. The exact total
     is 189 + 128 + 165 = 482, and the change is 500 − 482 = ₹18.
     If the rounded-up total were just over 500, we would have to work out the
     exact total. Now you try: ₹200 for items costing ₹48, ₹67 and ₹79. Enough?
     (yes: 50 + 70 + 80 = 200; exact 194)

D11. Estimate or exact? (a) How many people came to the Dussehra mela; (b) the
     change from ₹100 at a shop; (c) how much medicine to give; (d) how long the
     drive to Nani's house takes.
     Answer: (a) estimate; (b) exact; (c) exact; (d) estimate.
     Teacher explains: An estimate is enough when nobody needs the exact number
     and it would be hard to count: crowds, travel time, how much paint to buy.
     An exact answer is needed when money changes hands or when a mistake could
     be harmful, like medicine.
     Even when we need the exact answer, we still estimate first to check it.
     Now you try: the number of pages in a book you are buying for a friend who
     asked "is it a long book?" (estimate)

D12. Estimate 256 + 348 to the nearest hundred and to the nearest ten. Which
     estimate is closer to the exact answer?
     Answer: Nearest hundred: 300 + 300 = 600. Nearest ten: 260 + 350 = 610.
     Exact: 604, so the nearest-hundred estimate happens to be closer here.
     Teacher explains: Usually rounding to the smaller place (tens) gives the
     closer estimate. But sometimes the roundings cancel: 256 went UP by 44 and
     348 went DOWN by 48 when we rounded to hundreds, so the errors almost
     cancelled (600 is only 4 away from 604). To the nearest ten, both numbers
     went UP (by 4 and 2), so 610 is 6 too big.
     Estimates are not "right" or "wrong" — they are sensible or not. Now you
     try: estimate 149 + 151 to the nearest hundred. (100 + 200 = 300; exact
     300)

PART B — MATHEMATICAL ONLY
--------------------------
M1. Estimate by rounding each number to the nearest 10. Then give the exact
    answer in brackets.
    a) 47 + 32     b) 68 + 25     c) 91 − 38     d) 73 − 46
    e) 15 + 24     f) 85 + 66     g) 99 − 11     h) 54 − 45
    i) 123 + 58    j) 276 − 94    k) 38 + 38     l) 150 − 67
    m) 44 + 44     n) 705 − 298   o) 65 + 35     p) 812 − 409
    Answers: a) 50 + 30 = 80 (79)   b) 70 + 30 = 100 (93)   c) 90 − 40 = 50 (53)
             d) 70 − 50 = 20 (27)   e) 20 + 20 = 40 (39)
             f) 90 + 70 = 160 (151)   g) 100 − 10 = 90 (88)   h) 50 − 50 = 0 (9)
             i) 120 + 60 = 180 (181)   j) 280 − 90 = 190 (182)
             k) 40 + 40 = 80 (76)   l) 150 − 70 = 80 (83)   m) 40 + 40 = 80 (88)
             n) 710 − 300 = 410 (407)   o) 70 + 40 = 110 (100)
             p) 810 − 410 = 400 (403)

M2. Estimate by rounding each number to the nearest 100. Then give the exact
    answer in brackets.
    a) 487 + 312     b) 614 + 279
    c) 912 − 389     d) 745 − 268
    e) 256 + 348     f) 831 − 152
    g) 149 + 151     h) 650 + 249
    i) 903 − 497     j) 1234 + 567
    k) 2050 − 1480   l) 399 + 399
    m) 777 − 333     n) 1860 + 2145
    o) 5010 − 2990   p) 450 + 550
    Answers: a) 500 + 300 = 800 (799)   b) 600 + 300 = 900 (893)
             c) 900 − 400 = 500 (523)   d) 700 − 300 = 400 (477)
             e) 300 + 300 = 600 (604)   f) 800 − 200 = 600 (679)
             g) 100 + 200 = 300 (300)   h) 700 + 200 = 900 (899)
             i) 900 − 500 = 400 (406)   j) 1200 + 600 = 1800 (1801)
             k) 2100 − 1500 = 600 (570)   l) 400 + 400 = 800 (798)
             m) 800 − 300 = 500 (444)   n) 1900 + 2100 = 4000 (4005)
             o) 5000 − 3000 = 2000 (2020)   p) 500 + 600 = 1100 (1000)

M3. Estimate by rounding each number to the nearest 1000. Then give the exact
    answer in brackets.
    a) 23,650 + 18,420
    b) 35,280 − 12,910
    c) 4567 + 3499
    d) 8123 − 2876
    e) 12,500 + 7400
    f) 60,050 − 19,700
    g) 9999 + 1001
    h) 45,678 + 23,456
    i) 70,200 − 35,800
    j) 3333 + 6666
    k) 15,490 − 4510
    l) 99,500 − 49,500
    Answers: a) 24,000 + 18,000 = 42,000 (42,070)
             b) 35,000 − 13,000 = 22,000 (22,370)   c) 5000 + 3000 = 8000 (8066)
             d) 8000 − 3000 = 5000 (5247)   e) 13,000 + 7000 = 20,000 (19,900)
             f) 60,000 − 20,000 = 40,000 (40,350)
             g) 10,000 + 1000 = 11,000 (11,000)
             h) 46,000 + 23,000 = 69,000 (69,134)
             i) 70,000 − 36,000 = 34,000 (34,400)
             j) 3000 + 7000 = 10,000 (9999)   k) 15,000 − 5000 = 10,000 (10,980)
             l) 100,000 − 50,000 = 50,000 (50,000)

M4. Front-end estimate: keep only the first digit of each number (change the
    other digits to 0). Then give the exact answer in brackets.
    a) 3456 + 2781   b) 4210 + 3605
    c) 789 + 612     d) 5902 − 2150
    e) 8765 − 4321   f) 315 + 492
    g) 6099 + 2999   h) 947 − 318
    i) 7450 − 1980   j) 2345 + 4567
    k) 61 + 28       l) 93 − 47
    Answers: a) 3000 + 2000 = 5000 (6237)   b) 4000 + 3000 = 7000 (7815)
             c) 700 + 600 = 1300 (1401)   d) 5000 − 2000 = 3000 (3752)
             e) 8000 − 4000 = 4000 (4444)   f) 300 + 400 = 700 (807)
             g) 6000 + 2000 = 8000 (9098)   h) 900 − 300 = 600 (629)
             i) 7000 − 1000 = 6000 (5470)   j) 2000 + 4000 = 6000 (6912)
             k) 60 + 20 = 80 (89)   l) 90 − 40 = 50 (46)

M5. Estimate: round each number to its leading digit (one non-zero digit), then
    multiply. Give the exact answer in brackets.
    a) 38 × 6     b) 73 × 4
    c) 52 × 7     d) 49 × 8
    e) 61 × 9     f) 27 × 3
    g) 85 × 5     h) 94 × 2
    i) 312 × 3    j) 478 × 4
    k) 705 × 6    l) 189 × 7
    m) 651 × 8    n) 2150 × 3
    o) 4890 × 2   p) 96 × 9
    Answers: a) 40 × 6 = 240 (228)   b) 70 × 4 = 280 (292)
             c) 50 × 7 = 350 (364)   d) 50 × 8 = 400 (392)
             e) 60 × 9 = 540 (549)   f) 30 × 3 = 90 (81)   g) 90 × 5 = 450 (425)
             h) 90 × 2 = 180 (188)   i) 300 × 3 = 900 (936)
             j) 500 × 4 = 2000 (1912)   k) 700 × 6 = 4200 (4230)
             l) 200 × 7 = 1400 (1323)   m) 700 × 8 = 5600 (5208)
             n) 2000 × 3 = 6000 (6450)   o) 5000 × 2 = 10,000 (9780)
             p) 100 × 9 = 900 (864)

M6. Estimate: round both numbers to the nearest 10, then multiply. Give the
    exact answer in brackets.
    a) 47 × 23   b) 62 × 19
    c) 38 × 41   d) 71 × 29
    e) 55 × 22   f) 88 × 12
    g) 33 × 33   h) 19 × 19
    i) 94 × 51   j) 26 × 48
    k) 67 × 71   l) 82 × 38
    Answers: a) 50 × 20 = 1000 (1081)   b) 60 × 20 = 1200 (1178)
             c) 40 × 40 = 1600 (1558)   d) 70 × 30 = 2100 (2059)
             e) 60 × 20 = 1200 (1210)   f) 90 × 10 = 900 (1056)
             g) 30 × 30 = 900 (1089)   h) 20 × 20 = 400 (361)
             i) 90 × 50 = 4500 (4794)   j) 30 × 50 = 1500 (1248)
             k) 70 × 70 = 4900 (4757)   l) 80 × 40 = 3200 (3116)

M7. Estimate with a compatible number (one that divides exactly, found from a
    known fact).
    a) 358 ÷ 6    b) 245 ÷ 8    c) 2395 ÷ 8   d) 143 ÷ 7
    e) 419 ÷ 6    f) 812 ÷ 9    g) 538 ÷ 9    h) 275 ÷ 4
    i) 1210 ÷ 3   j) 6350 ÷ 7   k) 4180 ÷ 6   l) 99 ÷ 5
    m) 362 ÷ 4    n) 733 ÷ 8    o) 4812 ÷ 5   p) 1598 ÷ 2
    Answers: a) 360 ÷ 6 = 60   b) 240 ÷ 8 = 30   c) 2400 ÷ 8 = 300
             d) 140 ÷ 7 = 20   e) 420 ÷ 6 = 70   f) 810 ÷ 9 = 90
             g) 540 ÷ 9 = 60   h) 280 ÷ 4 = 70   i) 1200 ÷ 3 = 400
             j) 6300 ÷ 7 = 900   k) 4200 ÷ 6 = 700   l) 100 ÷ 5 = 20
             m) 360 ÷ 4 = 90   n) 720 ÷ 8 = 90   o) 5000 ÷ 5 = 1000
             p) 1600 ÷ 2 = 800

M8. Find the range: use the tens just below and just above the 2-digit number.
    Give the exact answer in brackets.
    a) 38 × 6 is between __ and __   b) 73 × 4 is between __ and __
    c) 52 × 7 is between __ and __   d) 49 × 8 is between __ and __
    e) 61 × 9 is between __ and __   f) 27 × 3 is between __ and __
    g) 85 × 5 is between __ and __   h) 14 × 6 is between __ and __
    i) 96 × 2 is between __ and __   j) 23 × 9 is between __ and __
    k) 58 × 5 is between __ and __   l) 31 × 8 is between __ and __
    Answers: a) 180 and 240 (228)   b) 280 and 320 (292)   c) 350 and 420 (364)
             d) 320 and 400 (392)   e) 540 and 630 (549)   f) 60 and 90 (81)
             g) 400 and 450 (425)   h) 60 and 120 (84)   i) 180 and 200 (192)
             j) 180 and 270 (207)   k) 250 and 300 (290)   l) 240 and 320 (248)

M9. Is the answer reasonable? Estimate to decide. If not, give the right answer
    in brackets.
    a) 398 + 207 = 905      b) 52 × 7 = 3164        c) 612 − 289 = 423
    d) 8 × 49 = 392         e) 4500 + 3700 = 8200   f) 903 − 98 = 5
    g) 67 × 3 = 201         h) 1200 ÷ 4 = 3000      i) 356 + 489 = 845
    j) 701 − 399 = 1102     k) 25 × 40 = 100        l) 810 ÷ 9 = 90
    m) 2468 + 1357 = 3825   n) 96 × 5 = 408         o) 5000 − 1 = 4999
    p) 640 ÷ 8 = 8
    Answers: a) no (605)   b) no (364)   c) no (323)   d) yes   e) yes
             f) no (805)   g) yes   h) no (300)   i) yes   j) no (302)
             k) no (1000)   l) yes   m) yes   n) no (480)   o) yes   p) no (80)

M10. Write > or < by estimating (no exact working needed).
     a) 47 + 38 __ 100       b) 93 − 48 __ 50        c) 6 × 39 __ 200
     d) 612 + 289 __ 1000    e) 503 − 198 __ 300     f) 7 × 61 __ 400
     g) 251 + 249 __ 400     h) 8 × 98 __ 800        i) 999 − 499 __ 400
     j) 4 × 26 __ 100        k) 72 + 29 __ 90        l) 1500 − 480 __ 1000
     m) 3 × 333 __ 1000      n) 55 + 55 __ 100       o) 9 × 11 __ 100
     p) 640 ÷ 8 __ 100
     Answers: a) <   b) <   c) >   d) <   e) >   f) >   g) >   h) <   i) >
              j) >   k) >   l) >   m) <   n) >   o) <   p) <

M11. Round each price UP to the next 10. Is the money enough? Give the exact
     total in brackets.
     a) ₹189 + ₹128 + ₹165 with ₹500
     b) ₹48 + ₹67 + ₹79 with ₹200
     c) ₹215 + ₹340 + ₹99 with ₹700
     d) ₹72 + ₹18 + ₹55 with ₹150
     e) ₹399 + ₹249 + ₹149 with ₹800
     f) ₹123 + ₹234 + ₹345 with ₹700
     g) ₹61 + ₹61 + ₹61 with ₹200
     h) ₹450 + ₹275 + ₹180 with ₹900
     i) ₹99 + ₹199 + ₹299 with ₹600
     j) ₹31 + ₹42 + ₹53 with ₹130
     k) ₹505 + ₹250 + ₹125 with ₹900
     l) ₹88 + ₹77 + ₹66 with ₹250
     Answers: a) 190 + 130 + 170 = 490, enough (482)
              b) 50 + 70 + 80 = 200, enough (194)
              c) 220 + 340 + 100 = 660, enough (654)
              d) 80 + 20 + 60 = 160, estimate too big, exact is enough (145)
              e) 400 + 250 + 150 = 800, enough (797)
              f) 130 + 240 + 350 = 720, not enough (702)
              g) 70 + 70 + 70 = 210, estimate too big, exact is enough (183)
              h) 450 + 280 + 180 = 910, not enough (905)
              i) 100 + 200 + 300 = 600, enough (597)
              j) 40 + 50 + 60 = 150, estimate too big, exact is enough (126)
              k) 510 + 250 + 130 = 890, enough (880)
              l) 90 + 80 + 70 = 240, enough (231)

M12. Round the 2-digit numbers to the nearest 10 and estimate. Is the estimate
     OVER or UNDER the exact answer?
     a) 47 + 38   b) 42 + 31   c) 85 − 23   d) 66 + 33
     e) 72 − 48   f) 24 × 3    g) 36 × 4    h) 51 + 22
     i) 98 − 11   j) 65 + 75   k) 19 × 5    l) 43 − 16
     Answers: a) 50 + 40 = 90, over (85)   b) 40 + 30 = 70, under (73)
              c) 90 − 20 = 70, over (62)   d) 70 + 30 = 100, over (99)
              e) 70 − 50 = 20, under (24)   f) 20 × 3 = 60, under (72)
              g) 40 × 4 = 160, over (144)   h) 50 + 20 = 70, under (73)
              i) 100 − 10 = 90, over (87)   j) 70 + 80 = 150, over (140)
              k) 20 × 5 = 100, over (95)   l) 40 − 20 = 20, under (27)

TEACHING TIPS
  Struggling: Practise rounding on a number line first (which ten or hundred is
  nearer?). Estimate with money and shopping bills, where "about" makes sense.
  Always ask "about how big should the answer be?" before calculating, and say
  how many digits the answer will have. Harder: estimate products of 3-digit by
  2-digit numbers (412 × 38 ≈ 400 × 40); give a range for a division; decide
  when rounding up or down is safer; find numbers where two different estimates
  disagree; check calculator answers in a whole shopping list.


================================================================================
TOPIC 4.42 — MULTI-STEP WORD PROBLEMS WITH BAR MODELS (TAPE DIAGRAMS)
================================================================================
WHAT THE CHILD MUST LEARN
  - Solve word problems in four steps: READ (what do we know, what do we want?),
    DRAW a bar model, SOLVE step by step, CHECK (estimate, and put the answer
    back into the story).
  - PART-WHOLE bar: the whole bar is split into parts; whole = part + part; a
    missing part = whole − the known parts.
  - COMPARISON bars: draw one bar for each person, lined up on the left; the
    extra piece is the DIFFERENCE. "27 more than 48" is 48 + 27.
  - EQUAL-PARTS bar: n equal boxes; whole = n × one part; one part = whole ÷ n.
    "3 times as many" means 3 equal boxes against 1 box, so the two together are
    4 boxes.
  - FRACTION bar: split the whole into equal parts (quarters, thirds ...). 1/4
    of 240 = 240 ÷ 4 = 60; 3/4 of 240 = 3 × 60 = 180.
  - Multi-step problems hide a middle number: find it first (for example the
    total cost before finding the change). Write ONE expression for the whole
    problem with brackets: (750 + 125) ÷ 5 = 175.
  - Working BACKWARDS: when the start is unknown, undo each step from the end,
    using the opposite operation.
  - Check every answer: is it sensible (estimate)? Does it fit the story when
    you put it back? Write the answer with its unit (₹, kg, m).

PART A — DESCRIPTIVE
--------------------
D1. A school library had 1250 books. It bought 375 new books, and then gave 480
    books to a village school. How many books does it have now?
       +------------------------+----------+
       |          1250          |   375    |
       +------------------------+----------+
       <---------------- ? ---------------->
       then take away 480 from the whole bar
    Answer: 1145 books. (1250 + 375) − 480 = 1625 − 480 = 1145.
    Teacher explains: Step 1 (the hidden middle number): books after
    buying = 1250 + 375 = 1625. Step 2: books after giving = 1625 − 480 = 1145.
    One expression: 1250 + 375 − 480 = 1145. Estimate to check:
    1300 + 400 − 500 = 1200, close to 1145.
    Common mistake: stopping after step 1. Ask "Have I answered the question
    that was asked?" Now you try: a shop had 860 eggs, got 240 more and sold
    575. How many are left? (525)

D2. Ravi has 48 stamps. Priya has 27 more stamps than Ravi. How many stamps do
    they have altogether?
       Ravi  |      48      |
       Priya |      48      |   27   |
    Answer: 123 stamps. Priya: 48 + 27 = 75. Together: 48 + 75 = 123.
    Teacher explains: Draw the bars lined up on the left. Priya's bar is the
    same as Ravi's plus an extra piece of 27. Step 1: Priya = 48 + 27 = 75. Step
    2: together = 48 + 75 = 123. Or from the picture: two 48-bars and one
    27-piece: 2 × 48 + 27 = 123.
    Common mistake: answering 75 (Priya only) or 48 + 27 = 75 as the total. The
    picture shows three pieces. Now you try: Anu has 65 shells and Meena has 18
    fewer. How many together? (112)

D3. Anu has 3 times as many marbles as Sonu. Together they have 96 marbles. How
    many does each have?
       Sonu |   ?   |
       Anu  |   ?   |   ?   |   ?   |     together: 96
    Answer: Sonu has 24 and Anu has 72.
    Teacher explains: "3 times as many" means Anu's bar is 3 boxes the size of
    Sonu's one box. Together there are 4 equal boxes, worth 96. One box:
    96 ÷ 4 = 24, so Sonu has 24. Anu: 3 × 24 = 72. Check: 24 + 72 = 96 (yes) and
    72 is 3 times 24 (yes).
    Common mistake: dividing 96 by 3. Count ALL the boxes in the picture. Now
    you try: together 60, and one has 4 times as many as the other. (12 and 48)

D4. Ravi and Meena have ₹150 altogether. Ravi has ₹30 more than Meena. How much
    does each have?
       Meena |     ?      |
       Ravi  |     ?      |  30  |     together: 150
    Answer: Meena has ₹60 and Ravi has ₹90.
    Teacher explains: Take the extra ₹30 away first: then the two bars are
    EQUAL. 150 − 30 = 120, and 120 ÷ 2 = 60, so Meena has ₹60. Ravi has
    60 + 30 = 90. One expression: (150 − 30) ÷ 2 = 60. Check: 60 + 90 = 150
    (yes), and 90 − 60 = 30 (yes).
    Common mistake: 150 ÷ 2 = 75 each — but then they would have the same
    amount. Now you try: total 100, difference 20. (40 and 60)

D5. 5 friends buy a cricket bat for ₹750 and a ball for ₹125. They share the
    cost equally. How much does each friend pay?
       +--------------------------+--------+
       |           750            |  125   |
       +--------------------------+--------+
       <-------------- whole -------------->
       +------+------+------+------+------+
       |  ?   |  ?   |  ?   |  ?   |  ?   |
       +------+------+------+------+------+
    Answer: ₹175 each. (750 + 125) ÷ 5 = 875 ÷ 5 = 175.
    Teacher explains: Step 1: the whole cost: 750 + 125 = 875. Step 2: share
    into 5 equal parts: 875 ÷ 5 = 175. The brackets say "add first". Without
    brackets, 750 + 125 ÷ 5 would mean 750 + 25 = 775 — a different problem!
    Check: 5 × 175 = 875 (yes).
    Now you try: 4 friends share a ₹360 pizza and ₹40 drinks equally. (₹100
    each)

D6. Riya had ₹240. She spent 1/4 of it on a book, and then ₹90 on a gift. How
    much money is left?
       +--------+--------+--------+--------+
       |  book  |        |        |        |
       +--------+--------+--------+--------+
       <--------------- 240 --------------->
    Answer: ₹90. The book cost 240 ÷ 4 = 60; left: 240 − 60 − 90 = 90.
    Teacher explains: Split the bar into 4 equal parts because of the quarter.
    One part: 240 ÷ 4 = 60, so the book cost ₹60. After the book:
    240 − 60 = 180. After the gift: 180 − 90 = 90. One expression:
    240 − 240 ÷ 4 − 90 = 90.
    Common mistake: taking 1/4 of the money AFTER the gift. Follow the story in
    order. Now you try: ₹300, spent 1/3 and then ₹50. (₹150)

D7. A farmer had 360 mangoes. He sold 2/3 of them in the market and gave half of
    the rest to his neighbours. How many are left?
       +----------+----------+----------+
       |   sold   |   sold   |   rest   |
       +----------+----------+----------+
       <------------- 360 -------------->
    Answer: 60 mangoes. Sold 240; rest 120; half of 120 is 60, so 60 are left.
    Teacher explains: Thirds: split 360 into 3 equal parts: 360 ÷ 3 = 120 in
    each. Sold 2 parts: 2 × 120 = 240. The rest is 1 part: 120. Half of the rest
    goes to neighbours: 120 ÷ 2 = 60, so 60 are left.
    Draw the last part again and split it in half to see it. Now you try: 240
    eggs, 3/4 sold, then 20 used. How many left? (40)

D8. Mother buys 3 kg of apples at ₹120 per kg and 2 dozen bananas at ₹60 per
    dozen. She pays with a ₹500 note. How much change does she get?
       +-------+-------+-------+-----+-----+--------+
       |  120  |  120  |  120  |  60 |  60 | change |
       +-------+-------+-------+-----+-----+--------+
       <------------------- 500 -------------------->
    Answer: ₹20. 3 × 120 + 2 × 60 = 360 + 120 = 480; 500 − 480 = 20.
    Teacher explains: Step 1: apples 3 × 120 = 360. Step 2: bananas
    2 × 60 = 120. Step 3: total 360 + 120 = 480. Step 4: change 500 − 480 = 20.
    One expression: 500 − (3 × 120 + 2 × 60) = 20. Estimate: 480 is just under
    500, so a small change is sensible.
    Now you try: 2 kg of sugar at ₹45 per kg and 3 packets of tea at ₹80 each,
    paid with ₹500. (₹170 change)

D9. A rope is 12 m long. Four pieces, each 2 m 25 cm long, are cut from it. How
    much rope is left?
       +-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-------+
       | 2 m 25 cm | 2 m 25 cm | 2 m 25 cm | 2 m 25 cm |   ?   |
       +-----------+-----------+-----------+-----------+-------+
       <------------------------ 12 m ------------------------->
    Answer: 3 m. 4 × 2 m 25 cm = 9 m; 12 m − 9 m = 3 m.
    Teacher explains: Change to one unit: 2 m 25 cm = 225 cm. Four pieces:
    4 × 225 = 900 cm, which is 9 m. Left: 12 m − 9 m = 3 m
    (1200 cm − 900 cm = 300 cm). Always use the same unit before adding or
    subtracting.
    Now you try: a 5 L bottle of water fills 6 glasses of 250 mL. How much is
    left? (3 L 500 mL)

D10. Anu spent ₹85 at the fair. Then her uncle gave her ₹50. Now she has ₹200.
     How much did she have at the start?
        forwards:   start ? --[− 85]--> ? --[+ 50]--> 200
        backwards:  235 <--[+ 85]-- 150 <--[− 50]-- 200
     Answer: ₹235. Work backwards: 200 − 50 = 150, then 150 + 85 = 235.
     Teacher explains: We know the END, so undo the steps from the end. The last
     step was "+ 50", so take away 50: 150. Before that she spent 85, so add it
     back: 235. Check forwards: 235 − 85 = 150, 150 + 50 = 200 (yes). As a box
     equation: □ − 85 + 50 = 200.
     Now you try: Ravi gave away 15 marbles, then won 22, and now has 60. How
     many at the start? (53)

D11. A train has 8 coaches with 72 seats in each. At station A, 390 people get
     on. At station B, 115 get off and 208 get on. How many seats are empty now?
        +--------------------------+----------+
        |   people on the train    | empty ?  |
        +--------------------------+----------+
        <----------- 8 × 72 seats ------------>
     Answer: 93 seats. Seats: 8 × 72 = 576. People: 390 − 115 + 208 = 483.
     Empty: 576 − 483 = 93.
     Teacher explains: Four steps: (1) all seats: 8 × 72 = 576; (2) after B,
     people off: 390 − 115 = 275; (3) people on: 275 + 208 = 483; (4) empty
     seats: 576 − 483 = 93. Write each step on its own line with a label, so
     nothing gets lost. Estimate: about 580 seats and about 480 people, so about
     100 empty (yes).
     Now you try: a bus has 52 seats; 38 get on, 12 get off, 19 get on. How many
     seats are empty? (7)

D12. Write an expression for this bar model and find the ?. Then make up a story
     for (250 − 75) ÷ 5.
        +-------+-------+-------+-------+-------+-------+
        |■■■■■■■|■■■■■■■|       |       |       |       |
        +-------+-------+-------+-------+-------+-------+
        <--------------------- 480 --------------------->
        ? = the two shaded parts
     Answer: 480 ÷ 6 × 2 = 160. A story: "Riya had ₹250. She spent ₹75 and
     shared the rest equally among 5 friends. How much did each get?" (₹35)
     Teacher explains: The whole, 480, is in 6 equal parts, so one part is
     480 ÷ 6 = 80, and two parts are 2 × 80 = 160. For the story, read the
     expression from the inside: the brackets say "250 take away 75" first
     (175), then "÷ 5" means share into 5 equal parts: 175 ÷ 5 = 35.
     Making up stories for expressions shows that the child really understands
     them. Now you try: write the expression for 4 equal boxes of 45 and one
     extra box of 20. (4 × 45 + 20 = 200)

PART B — MATHEMATICAL ONLY
--------------------------
M1. Work out. Brackets first.
    a) (250 − 75) ÷ 5       b) (750 + 125) ÷ 5      c) (150 − 30) ÷ 2
    d) 96 ÷ 4 × 3           e) (48 + 27) + 48       f) 500 − (3 × 120)
    g) 4 × 45 + 20          h) (360 + 40) ÷ 4       i) 240 − 240 ÷ 4
    j) (100 − 20) ÷ 2       k) 12 × 5 − 18          l) (63 + 37) × 4
    m) 1000 − 4 × 125       n) (81 − 9) ÷ 8         o) 7 × 8 + 7 × 2
    p) (56 ÷ 8) × 11        q) 300 − (45 + 55)      r) (1250 + 375) − 480
    s) (200 − 50) + 85      t) 480 ÷ 6 × 2
    Answers: a) 35   b) 175   c) 60   d) 72   e) 123   f) 140   g) 200   h) 100
             i) 180   j) 40   k) 42   l) 400   m) 500   n) 9   o) 70   p) 77
             q) 200   r) 1145   s) 235   t) 160

M2. Three and four steps. Brackets first, then × and ÷, then + and −.
    a) 500 − (3 × 120 + 2 × 60)     b) 8 × 72 − (390 − 115 + 208)
    c) (360 − 240) ÷ 2              d) (96 ÷ 4) × 3 + 96 ÷ 4
    e) 1000 − (250 + 175 + 325)     f) (45 + 55) × (12 − 7)
    g) (600 − 150) ÷ 3 + 50         h) 2 × 48 + 27
    i) (5 × 60 + 4 × 25) ÷ 8        j) (144 ÷ 12) × (36 ÷ 9)
    k) 750 − 2 × 125 − 3 × 50       l) (1200 − 300) ÷ 9 × 2
    m) (52 − 38 + 12 − 19) + 45     n) 4 × (225 + 75) − 1000
    o) (75 + 25) × 3 − 150          p) (900 ÷ 3) − (900 ÷ 4)
    Answers: a) 20   b) 93   c) 60   d) 96   e) 250   f) 500   g) 200   h) 123
             i) 50   j) 48   k) 350   l) 200   m) 52   n) 200   o) 150   p) 75

M3. Part-whole bar models. Find the ?.
    a)
       +-------------+-----------+------+
       |     180     |    145    |  ?   |
       +-------------+-----------+------+
       <------------- 500 -------------->
    b)
       +------+-------------+
       |  ?   |     275     |
       +------+-------------+
       <------- 800 -------->
    c)
       +----------+----------+------------------+
       |   125    |   125    |       250        |
       +----------+----------+------------------+
       <------------------ ? ------------------->
    d)
       +-----------+------+--------+------+
       |     90    |  ?   |   60   |  45  |
       +-----------+------+--------+------+
       <-------------- 300 --------------->
    e)
       +----------------------+--------+------+
       |         1250         |  375   |  ?   |
       +----------------------+--------+------+
       <---------------- 2000 ---------------->
    f)
       +------+--------------------------------+
       |  ?   |              999               |
       +------+--------------------------------+
       <---------------- 1000 ----------------->
    g)
       +---------+---------+------------------+
       |    48   |    52   |       100        |
       +---------+---------+------------------+
       <----------------- ? ------------------>
    h)
       +------+------------+--------+
       |  ?   |    320     |  180   |
       +------+------------+--------+
       <----------- 1000 ----------->
    Answers: a) 500 − (180 + 145) = 175   b) 800 − 275 = 525
             c) 125 + 125 + 250 = 500   d) 300 − (90 + 60 + 45) = 105
             e) 2000 − (1250 + 375) = 375   f) 1000 − 999 = 1
             g) 48 + 52 + 100 = 200   h) 1000 − (320 + 180) = 500

M4. Comparison bar models. The extra piece is the difference. Find what is
    asked.
    a)
       A |     48     |
       B |     48     |  27  |    A + B = ?
    b)
       A |     ?      |  18  |    A = 65
       B |     ?      |    A + B = ?
    c)
       A |    ?     |
       B |    ?     |  30  |    A + B = 150
    d)
       A |    250     |
       B |    250     |  75  |    B = ?
    e)
       A |     ?      |  45  |    A = 120
       B |     ?      |    B = ?
    f)
       A |    ?     |
       B |    ?     |  20  |    A + B = 100
    g)
       A |    340     |
       B |    340     | 160  |    A + B = ?
    h)
       A |    ?     |
       B |    ?     |  64  |    A + B = 500
    Answers: a) 48 + 48 + 27 = 123   b) 65 + (65 − 18) = 112
             c) A = (150 − 30) ÷ 2 = 60, B = 90   d) 250 + 75 = 325
             e) 120 − 45 = 75   f) A = (100 − 20) ÷ 2 = 40, B = 60
             g) 340 + 340 + 160 = 840   h) A = (500 − 64) ÷ 2 = 218, B = 282

M5. Equal-parts bar models. Find the ?.
    a)
       +-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+
       |  ?  |  ?  |  ?  |  ?  |  ?  |
       +-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+
       <------------ 875 ------------>
    b)
       +-----+-----+-----+-----+
       |  45 |  45 |  45 |  45 |
       +-----+-----+-----+-----+
       <---------- ? ---------->
    c)
       +-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+
       |  ?  |  ?  |  ?  |  ?  |  ?  |  ?  |
       +-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+
       <--------------- 480 --------------->
    d)
       +-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+
       |  72 |  72 |  72 |  72 |  72 |  72 |  72 |  72 |
       +-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+
       <---------------------- ? ---------------------->
    e)
       +-----+-----+-----+
       |  ?  |  ?  |  ?  |
       +-----+-----+-----+
       <------ 96 ------->
    f)
       +-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+
       | 125 | 125 | 125 | 125 | 125 | 125 | 125 |
       +-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+
       <------------------- ? ------------------->
    g)
       +-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+
       |  ?  |  ?  |  ?  |  ?  |  ?  |  ?  |  ?  |  ?  |  ?  |
       +-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+
       <----------------------- 1080 ------------------------>
    h)
       +-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+
       |  36 |  36 |  36 |  36 |  36 |  36 |  36 |  36 |  36 |  36 |
       +-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+
       <---------------------------- ? ---------------------------->
    Answers: a) 875 ÷ 5 = 175   b) 4 × 45 = 180   c) 480 ÷ 6 = 80
             d) 8 × 72 = 576   e) 96 ÷ 3 = 32   f) 7 × 125 = 875
             g) 1080 ÷ 9 = 120   h) 10 × 36 = 360

M6. Fraction bar models. Find the shaded amount and the amount not shaded.
    a) shaded = 1/4 of 240
       +-----+-----+-----+-----+
       |■■■■■|     |     |     |
       +-----+-----+-----+-----+
       <--------- 240 --------->
    b) shaded = 2/3 of 360
       +-----+-----+-----+
       |■■■■■|■■■■■|     |
       +-----+-----+-----+
       <------ 360 ------>
    c) shaded = 3/5 of 500
       +-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+
       |■■■■■|■■■■■|■■■■■|     |     |
       +-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+
       <------------ 500 ------------>
    d) shaded = 2/6 of 480
       +-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+
       |■■■■■|■■■■■|     |     |     |     |
       +-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+
       <--------------- 480 --------------->
    e) shaded = 5/8 of 720
       +-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+
       |■■■■■|■■■■■|■■■■■|■■■■■|■■■■■|     |     |     |
       +-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+
       <--------------------- 720 --------------------->
    f) shaded = 1/3 of 90
       +-----+-----+-----+
       |■■■■■|     |     |
       +-----+-----+-----+
       <------ 90 ------->
    g) shaded = 3/4 of 1000
       +-----+-----+-----+-----+
       |■■■■■|■■■■■|■■■■■|     |
       +-----+-----+-----+-----+
       <-------- 1000 --------->
    h) shaded = 4/7 of 630
       +-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+
       |■■■■■|■■■■■|■■■■■|■■■■■|     |     |     |
       +-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+
       <------------------ 630 ------------------>
    Answers: a) shaded 240 ÷ 4 × 1 = 60, not shaded 180
             b) shaded 360 ÷ 3 × 2 = 240, not shaded 120
             c) shaded 500 ÷ 5 × 3 = 300, not shaded 200
             d) shaded 480 ÷ 6 × 2 = 160, not shaded 320
             e) shaded 720 ÷ 8 × 5 = 450, not shaded 270
             f) shaded 90 ÷ 3 × 1 = 30, not shaded 60
             g) shaded 1000 ÷ 4 × 3 = 750, not shaded 250
             h) shaded 630 ÷ 7 × 4 = 360, not shaded 270

M7. Write ONE expression for each bar model, then work it out.
    a) Find one ? (the five ? are equal).
       +------+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+
       |  75  |  ?  |  ?  |  ?  |  ?  |  ?  |
       +------+-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+
       <--------------- 250 ---------------->
    b) Find the whole.
       +-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+
       |  45 |  45 |  45 |  45 |  20 |
       +-----+-----+-----+-----+-----+
       <------------- ? ------------->
    c) Find one ?.
       +------+------+------+
       |  ?   |  ?   |  ?   |
       +------+------+------+
       <-------- 96 -------->
    d) Find the change.
       +------+------+------+----------+
       | 120  | 120  | 120  | change ? |
       +------+------+------+----------+
       <------------- 500 ------------->
    e) Find the whole.
       +----+----+----+----+----+----+----+
       | 60 | 60 | 60 | 60 | 60 | 60 | 60 |
       +----+----+----+----+----+----+----+
       <--------------- ? ---------------->
    f) Find the ?.
       +------------------+--------+
       |       300        |   ?    |
       +------------------+--------+
       <----------- 450 ----------->
    g) Find the shaded part.
       +------+------+------+------+
       |■■■■■■|■■■■■■|■■■■■■|      |
       +------+------+------+------+
       <----------- 840 ----------->
    h) Find the ?.
       +----+----+----+----+----+----+----+----+-----+
       | 38 | 38 | 38 | 38 | 38 | 38 | 38 | 38 |  ?  |
       +----+----+----+----+----+----+----+----+-----+
       <-------------------- 400 -------------------->
    Answers: a) (250 − 75) ÷ 5 = 35   b) 4 × 45 + 20 = 200   c) 96 ÷ 3 = 32
             d) 500 − 3 × 120 = 140   e) 7 × 60 = 420   f) 450 − 300 = 150
             g) 840 ÷ 4 × 3 = 630   h) 400 − 8 × 38 = 96

M8. Money problems written as expressions (in ₹).
    a) 500 − (3 × 120 + 2 × 60)    b) 200 − (48 + 67 + 79)
    c) 1000 − (4 × 125 + 250)      d) 100 − 3 × 25
    e) 2000 − (1250 + 375 + 199)   f) 500 − 2 × 45 − 3 × 80
    g) (750 + 125) ÷ 5             h) (360 + 40) ÷ 4
    i) 10 × 35 + 5 × 12            j) 1000 − 12 × 75
    k) (6 × 15) + (4 × 20) + 30    l) 500 − (189 + 128 + 165)
    m) (2 × 250 + 3 × 150) − 800   n) 5 × 99 + 5
    o) (1000 − 280) ÷ 8            p) 3 × 199 + 2 × 49
    Answers: a) ₹20   b) ₹6   c) ₹250   d) ₹25   e) ₹176   f) ₹170   g) ₹175
             h) ₹100   i) ₹410   j) ₹100   k) ₹200   l) ₹18   m) ₹150   n) ₹500
             o) ₹90   p) ₹695

M9. Measurement problems written as expressions. Change to one unit when you
    need to.
    a) 12 m − 4 × 2 m 25 cm        b) 5 L − 6 × 250 mL
    c) 3 kg − 4 × 350 g            d) 2 × 1 km 250 m + 800 m
    e) (3 h 30 min − 45 min) ÷ 3   f) 10 kg − 3 × 2 kg 500 g
    g) 1 L − 3 × 200 mL            h) 6 × 45 min + 30 min
    i) 4 × 1 m 50 cm − 2 m         j) (2 km − 500 m) ÷ 3
    k) 5 × 150 g + 250 g           l) 24 h − (8 h + 7 h 30 min)
    m) 3 × 1 L 250 mL − 750 mL     n) (60 cm + 90 cm) × 2
    o) 7 × 2 min 30 s              p) 1 km − 8 × 75 m
    Answers: a) 3 m   b) 3 L 500 mL   c) 1 kg 600 g   d) 3 km 300 m   e) 55 min
             f) 2 kg 500 g   g) 400 mL   h) 5 h   i) 4 m   j) 500 m   k) 1 kg
             l) 8 h 30 min   m) 3 L   n) 3 m   o) 17 min 30 s   p) 400 m

M10. Fractions of amounts, one and two steps.
     a) 1/4 of 240                   b) 3/4 of 240
     c) 2/3 of 360                   d) 360 − 2/3 of 360
     e) 240 − 1/4 of 240 − 90        f) 1/2 of (360 − 240)
     g) 3/5 of 500                   h) 500 − 3/5 of 500
     i) 1/3 of 300 + 50              j) 5/8 of 720
     k) 720 − 5/8 of 720             l) 1/10 of 1000 + 1/2 of 1000
     m) 2/3 of 90 + 1/3 of 90        n) 3/4 of 1000
     o) 1/6 of 480 × 2               p) 4/7 of 630
     Answers: a) 60   b) 180   c) 240   d) 120   e) 90   f) 60   g) 300   h) 200
              i) 150   j) 450   k) 270   l) 600   m) 90   n) 750   o) 160
              p) 360

M11. Work backwards to find the start number.
     a) □ − 85 + 50 = 200     b) □ − 15 + 22 = 60      c) □ + 40 − 25 = 100
     d) □ × 2 + 10 = 70       e) □ ÷ 3 − 5 = 20        f) □ − 120 − 80 = 300
     g) □ + 250 − 600 = 150   h) □ × 4 − 20 = 80       i) (□ − 30) ÷ 2 = 45
     j) (□ + 15) × 3 = 75     k) □ − 1/2 of 100 = 25   l) □ ÷ 5 + 12 = 30
     m) □ − 999 + 1 = 2       n) □ × 10 − 100 = 900    o) (□ + 5) ÷ 5 = 20
     p) □ − 75 − 75 = 50
     Answers: a) 235   b) 53   c) 85   d) 30   e) 75   f) 500   g) 500   h) 25
              i) 120   j) 10   k) 75   l) 90   m) 1000   n) 100   o) 95   p) 200

M12. Two numbers. Draw the bars, then find both numbers (smaller first).
     a) total 96, one is 3 times the other
     b) total 60, one is 4 times the other
     c) total 150, difference 30
     d) total 100, difference 20
     e) total 250, one is 4 times the other
     f) total 500, difference 64
     g) total 84, one is 6 times the other
     h) total 75, difference 15
     i) total 1000, one is 9 times the other
     j) total 36, difference 0
     k) total 120, one is 2 times the other
     l) total 210, difference 50
     Answers: a) 24 and 72   b) 12 and 48   c) 60 and 90   d) 40 and 60
              e) 50 and 200   f) 218 and 282   g) 12 and 72   h) 30 and 45
              i) 100 and 900   j) 18 and 18   k) 40 and 80   l) 80 and 130

TEACHING TIPS
  Struggling: Act the story out with coins, blocks or strips of paper before
  drawing. Draw every bar model with the ? marked, and label every number. Solve
  one step at a time and write what each number means ("480 = total cost").
  Start with two-step problems before three and four steps. Harder: write your
  own four-step problem for a friend; solve problems with three people ("A has
  twice B, B has 10 more than C, total 170"); turn a bar model into a story and
  an expression; problems with fractions of what is left; compare two ways of
  solving the same problem.


================================================================================
MIXED REVISION TEST — STAGE 4
================================================================================
Two parts with the same number of questions: Part A is descriptive (word
problems and "explain" questions, written with working), Part B is
mathematical only. 1 mark each, 60 marks in all. Spread it over a week.

PART A — DESCRIPTIVE, 30 marks
  1.  Write 4,07,250 in words using the Indian system, and in the
      international system with commas.
  2.  The population of a town is 46,837. Round it to the nearest thousand
      and to the nearest hundred. Which is more useful for a newspaper?
  3.  A school collected 47,856 in January and 29,467 in February. How many
      in all? Show the column working.
  4.  Explain how you can use 6 × 8 = 48 to find 6 × 80 and 60 × 80.
  5.  Show how the distributive property helps you work out 7 × 98 in your
      head.
  6.  A box holds 348 pencils. How many pencils are in 6 boxes?
  7.  Draw an area model for 57 × 43 and find the answer.
  8.  47 children must travel in cars that carry 5 children each. How many
      cars are needed? Explain what you did with the remainder.
  9.  A farmer packs 7,465 eggs into trays of 8. How many full trays? How
      many eggs are left over? Show the long division.
 10.  Explain why 56 ÷ 7 = 8 can be checked with a multiplication.
 11.  A giraffe is 5 m tall. A tree is 3 times as tall. How tall is the
      tree? How much taller is the tree than the giraffe?
 12.  Riya says 20 − 3 × 4 + 6 = 74. Is she right? Explain the correct
      order.
 13.  Find all the factor pairs of 36. Which factor is paired with itself?
 14.  Is 91 prime? Explain how you checked.
 15.  Without dividing, explain whether 5,742 is divisible by 3, by 6 and
      by 9.
 16.  Draw dot patterns to show why 1 + 3 + 5 + 7 = 16 is a square number.
 17.  Explain why we only need to cross out multiples of 2, 3, 5 and 7 to
      find all the primes up to 100.
 18.  A class has 32 children. 3/8 of them walk to school. How many walk?
 19.  Explain, with a fraction wall or strip, why 3/4 = 6/8.
 20.  Which is bigger, 5/6 or 7/9? Explain your method.
 21.  Is 7/12 closer to 0, 1/2 or 1? Explain using a benchmark.
 22.  A recipe uses 17/4 cups of flour. Write this as a mixed number and
      explain it with pictures of cups.
 23.  Aman ate 5/8 of a pizza and Neha ate 7/8 of another pizza of the same
      size. How much did they eat together? Write it as a mixed number.
 24.  Write ₹25.75 in rupees and paise, and explain what the 7 means.
 25.  Which is greater, 0.5 or 0.45? Explain the mistake a child makes
      when they think 0.45 is greater.
 26.  A rectangular garden is 12 m long and 7 m wide. Find its perimeter
      and its area. Explain why the units are different.
 27.  Find two rectangles with an area of 24 cm² but different perimeters.
 28.  A train leaves at 14:35 and arrives at 17:10. How long is the
      journey? Explain your method on a time line.
 29.  Draw a bar model: a shop had 250 mangoes, sold 75, and packed the
      rest equally into 5 boxes. How many mangoes in each box?
 30.  Estimate 398 × 21 and explain whether 8,358 is a reasonable answer.

PART B — MATHEMATICAL ONLY, 30 marks
 31.  Write in numerals: three hundred six thousand, forty.
 32.  Round 46,837 to the nearest 10 and the nearest 10,000.
 33.  60,003 − 27,458 = __
 34.  8 × 7 = __   9 × 6 = __   12 × 11 = __   7 × 12 = __
 35.  25 × 4 × 3 = __          6 × 34 = 6 × 30 + 6 × __
 36.  3,450 × 100 = __         72,000 ÷ 1000 = __
 37.  426 × 35 = __
 38.  54 ÷ 6 = __   84 ÷ 7 = __   61 ÷ 8 = __ remainder __
 39.  936 ÷ 18 = __
 40.  If 13 × 24 = 312, then 312 ÷ 24 = __ and 312 ÷ 13 = __
 41.  (15 + 9) ÷ 4 × 2 = __    20 − 3 × 4 + 6 = __
 42.  List all factors of 30.  Write the first five multiples of 7.
 43.  Which are prime: 21, 29, 37, 49, 51, 53?
 44.  Which of 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 9, 10 divide 1,260?
 45.  12² = __    The 6th triangular number = __
 46.  How many primes are there between 1 and 100?
 47.  2/5 of 35 = __     Mark 3/4 on a number line from 0 to 1 (describe)
 48.  2/3 = __/12 = 10/__     Simplify 18/24.
 49.  Put in order, smallest first: 3/4, 2/3, 5/8, 1/2
 50.  Closer to 0, 1/2 or 1?  1/9 ; 5/9 ; 8/9
 51.  23/5 = __ (mixed)        4 2/7 = __ (improper)
 52.  5/8 + 7/8 = __           4 − 3/5 = __
 53.  5 × 2/3 = __              3/4 × 20 = __
 54.  0.7 = __/10    0.35 = __/100    43/100 = __ (decimal)
 55.  Put < , > or =:  0.5 __ 0.45    1.09 __ 1.1    0.30 __ 0.3
 56.  XLIX = __       1984 in Roman numerals = __
 57.  Perimeter of a regular hexagon with side 9 cm = __
 58.  Area of a rectangle 15 cm by 8 cm = __ ;  of a square with side 11 m
 59.  3 km 250 m = __ m    2 h 15 min = __ min    4.5 kg = __ g
 60.  Find □:  □ × 8 = 56    3 × □ + 4 = 19    □ ÷ 6 = 12

ANSWER KEY
  Part A (accept any clear, correct method)
   1. four lakh seven thousand two hundred fifty; 407,250
   2. 47,000 ; 46,800 ; nearest thousand is usually enough for a headline
   3. 77,323
   4. 6 × 80 is 10 times 48 = 480; 60 × 80 is 100 times 48 = 4,800
   5. 7 × 98 = 7 × 100 − 7 × 2 = 700 − 14 = 686
   6. 2,088 pencils
   7. 57 × 43: 50 × 40 = 2000, 50 × 3 = 150, 7 × 40 = 280, 7 × 3 = 21;
      total 2,451
   8. 10 cars (47 ÷ 5 = 9 remainder 2; the 2 children still need a car,
      so round up)
   9. 933 full trays, 1 egg left over
  10. 7 × 8 = 56, so dividing 56 into 7 groups gives 8 in each
  11. 15 m; 10 m taller
  12. No. Multiply first: 3 × 4 = 12; then left to right: 20 − 12 = 8,
      8 + 6 = 14
  13. 1 × 36, 2 × 18, 3 × 12, 4 × 9, 6 × 6; 6 is paired with itself
  14. No: 91 = 7 × 13 (check primes 2, 3, 5, 7 up to about 10)
  15. digit sum 5 + 7 + 4 + 2 = 18: divisible by 3 and by 9; it is even
      and divisible by 3, so also by 6
  16. each odd number adds an "L" shaped layer to make the next square:
      1, 4, 9, 16
  17. any composite number up to 100 has a factor no bigger than 10, and
      the primes up to 10 are 2, 3, 5, 7
  18. 12 children
  19. each quarter is the same length as two eighths, so three quarters
      match six eighths
  20. 5/6 (5/6 = 15/18 and 7/9 = 14/18)
  21. closer to 1/2 (6/12 is a half, 7/12 is just more)
  22. 4 1/4 cups: 4 full cups and one quarter of a cup
  23. 12/8 = 1 4/8 = 1 1/2 pizzas
  24. 25 rupees 75 paise; the 7 means 7 tenths of a rupee (70 paise)
  25. 0.5 = 0.50 > 0.45; the child compares 45 with 5 instead of lining
      up the places
  26. perimeter 38 m; area 84 m²; perimeter measures length round the
      edge, area counts squares
  27. e.g. 4 × 6 (perimeter 20 cm) and 3 × 8 (perimeter 22 cm) (also
      2 × 12, 1 × 24)
  28. 2 h 35 min (14:35 → 17:00 is 2 h 25 min, then 10 min more)
  29. 35 mangoes in each box ((250 − 75) ÷ 5 = 175 ÷ 5)
  30. about 400 × 20 = 8,000; 8,358 is reasonable (exact 398 × 21 = 8,358)
  Part B
  31. 306,040                     32. 46,840 ; 50,000
  33. 32,545                      34. 56, 54, 132, 84
  35. 300 ; 4                     36. 345,000 ; 72
  37. 14,910                      38. 9, 12, 7 remainder 5
  39. 52                          40. 13 ; 24
  41. 12 ; 14                     42. 1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 10, 15, 30 ;
                                      7, 14, 21, 28, 35
  43. 29, 37, 53                  44. all of them (2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 9, 10)
  45. 144 ; 21                    46. 25
  47. 14 ; three of four equal parts from 0 towards 1
  48. 8 ; 15 ; 3/4                49. 1/2, 5/8, 2/3, 3/4
  50. 0 ; 1/2 ; 1                 51. 4 3/5 ; 30/7
  52. 1 1/2 (12/8) ; 3 2/5        53. 10/3 = 3 1/3 ; 15
  54. 7 ; 35 ; 0.43               55. > ; < ; =
  56. 49 ; MCMLXXXIV              57. 54 cm
  58. 120 cm² ; 121 m²            59. 3250 ; 135 ; 4500
  60. 7 ; 5 ; 72

Scoring guide (out of 60):
  51-60  Ready for Stage 5.
  39-50  Nearly there — practise the topics of the missed questions for
         two weeks, then try those questions again.
  0-38   Keep working through Stage 4 (revise Stage 3 if needed); test
         again in a month.
  Compare the two parts: strong in B but weak in A means the child needs
  more word problems, bar models and explaining; strong in A but weak in B
  means more fact and drill practice for speed and accuracy.


================================================================================
END OF STAGE 4 — READINESS CHECKLIST FOR STAGE 5
================================================================================
The child is ready for Stage 5 (Grades 5-6) when they can usually:
  [ ] Read, write, compare and order numbers to a million (and lakhs and
      crores), and give the value of every digit
  [ ] Round numbers to the nearest 10, 100, 1000 and use rounding to
      estimate
  [ ] Add and subtract multi-digit numbers with the column method and check
  [ ] Recall all multiplication facts to 12 × 12 and their division facts
  [ ] Use the commutative, associative and distributive properties to
      calculate cleverly
  [ ] Multiply and divide by 10, 100 and 1000
  [ ] Multiply up to 4-digit by 1-digit and 2-digit by 2-digit numbers
  [ ] Divide with remainders and use long division (1- and 2-digit
      divisors), interpreting the remainder
  [ ] Solve "times as many" comparison problems
  [ ] Use the order of operations with brackets
  [ ] Find factors, multiples, primes and composites; use divisibility
      rules; know square and triangular numbers
  [ ] Explain fractions as parts of a whole, of a set and as points on a
      number line
  [ ] Find equivalent fractions, simplify, compare and order fractions
  [ ] Convert between mixed numbers and improper fractions
  [ ] Add and subtract fractions with the same denominator; multiply a
      fraction by a whole number
  [ ] Read, write and compare decimals (tenths and hundredths) and link
      them to fractions and money
  [ ] Read and write Roman numerals
  [ ] Find perimeter and area of rectangles and composite shapes, and
      explain the difference between them
  [ ] Measure and classify angles; identify parallel and perpendicular
      lines; classify triangles and quadrilaterals
  [ ] Find lines of symmetry and reflect shapes
  [ ] Convert between units of length, mass, capacity and time
  [ ] Use the 24-hour clock and find elapsed time
  [ ] Read and make line plots, bar graphs and tables
  [ ] Find rules in input-output tables and number patterns
  [ ] Find unknowns in simple equations
  [ ] Estimate answers and check that they are reasonable
  [ ] Solve multi-step word problems using bar models

Fractions with different denominators, decimal operations, ratio,
percentages, negative numbers and beginning algebra are the main new work
of Stage 5.
================================================================================
